- **A, C, D** · Uses the literal language of doubling, which leaves open whether the sizes or the series are doubled.
  - A · RU «Ведь поскольку все интервалы завершаются в октахорде, а оттуда вновь удваиваются, [интервал] называется октавой [«через все»], потому что проходит через восемь струн. Доказательством служат и названия, доставшиеся следующим интервалам, каковы «через все» с «через четыре», «через все» с «через пять», «дважды через все», то есть октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и две октавы.» / EN “For because all the intervals are completed within the octachord, and from there are doubled over again, it is called the octave [‘through all’], because it passes through eight strings. The names that have fallen to the following intervals are also evidence, such as the ‘through all’ with the ‘through four’, the ‘through all’ with the ‘through five’, the ‘twice through all’, that is, the octave with a fourth, the octave with a fifth, and two octaves.”
  - C · RU «Ибо, так как все интервалы завершаются в октахорде и оттуда снова удваиваются, [этот интервал] называется диапазоном, поскольку проходит через восемь струн. Доказательством служат и наименования, доставшиеся последующим интервалам, каковы диапазон с диатессароном, диапазон с диапенте, дисдиапазон, то есть октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и две октавы.» / EN “For since all the intervals are completed in the octachord and from there are doubled again, it is called diapason, because it passes through eight strings. The names that fell to the subsequent intervals are also evidence of this, such as diapason with diatessaron, diapason with diapente, disdiapason, that is, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and two octaves.”
  - D · RU «Ведь все интервалы укладываются в октахорд, а затем удваиваются вновь. Поэтому интервал, проходящий через восемь струн, называют октавой [«через все»]. Об этом свидетельствуют и названия, доставшиеся следующим интервалам: октава с квартой, октава с квинтой, двойная октава, то есть «восьмая с четвёртой», «восьмая с пятой» и «две восьмых».» / EN “For all the intervals are completed within the octachord and thereafter doubled in turn. Hence the interval that passes through eight strings is called the octave [‘through all’]. Further evidence comes from the names given to the succeeding intervals, such as the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave, that is, ‘an eighth with a fourth’, ‘an eighth with a fifth’, and ‘two eighths’.”

**Verdict** · B makes explicit the contextual sense of geminare. The sequence starts again at the octave, as X29–X30 and X32 illustrate. 'Double' can mean reproduce in a second series, but suggests the interval sizes have changed.

- **Russian** · а затем повторяются
- **English** · and are then repeated from there
- **Evidence** · Lewis & Short, s.v. gemino, including facinus and consulatus, Corpus of Latin Dictionaries [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · modified

**Errors**

- **C · both · minor term** · “диапазоном … диапазон с диатессароном … дисдиапазон / diapason … diapason with diatessaron … disdiapason”. Unexplained Greek-form names displace the prescribed running terms and Russian «диапазон» now ordinarily denotes a range. **Fix** · Use the established interval names and give the literal senses alongside them or in a note. *(confirmed)*
- **B · both · minor omission** · “the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave, that is, an octave with a fourth, an octave with a fifth, and two octaves”. The translation gives the required terms but the note supplies a literal gloss only for disdiapason, leaving the first two apparent tautologies unexplained. **Fix** · Keep the prescribed interval names and explain the two remaining Greek-derived names in the note. *(confirmed)*
- **D · both · minor language** · “two eighths / две восьмых”. As running musical prose the literal gloss can suggest fractions, especially in Russian. **Fix** · Use ‘two octaves’ / «две октавы» and explain the etymology in a note. *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · Explain the two sets of interval names, the through-all etymology and the marginal cross-reference to annotations without supplying their contents.
- **D** · The three compound intervals span eleven, twelve and fifteen note positions because the shared endpoint of two joined intervals counts once.

### X31 · Xyl. 44.21–45.2

*Consonant autem diatessaron dictum intervallum, et diapente, secundum id genus consonantiae quod Paraphonum Graeci dicunt, quasi iuxta sonans. Diapason autem, diapason cum diatessaron, diapason cum diapente, et disdiapason, secundum id genus consonantiae quod Antiphonum appellant, quasi contra sonans.*

The fourth and fifth belong to the Greek class paraphonon, glossed as sounding alongside. The octave, both compounds with it and the double octave belong to antiphonon, glossed as sounding opposite.

- **Russian base** · D. Idiomatic Russian preserves the parallel classification and the literal glosses.
- **English base** · D. Clear and faithful classification with natural glosses. It distributes the naming participle over both intervals, which does not alter the classification.

**Divergence · diatessaron dictum intervallum, et diapente**

What does dictum describe?

- **A, B, D** · Takes dictum as naming the interval, with B and D distributing the naming construction over the two intervals in translation.
  - A · RU «Консонируют же интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта по тому роду консонанса, который греки называют парафонным, как бы «звучащим рядом»; а октава, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по тому роду консонанса, который именуют антифонным, как бы «звучащим против».» / EN “Now the interval called the fourth, and the fifth, are consonant according to that kind of consonance which the Greeks call paraphonon, as it were ‘sounding beside’; while the octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave [are consonant] according to that kind of consonance which they call antiphonon, as it were ‘sounding against’.”
  - B · RU «Кварта и квинта консонируют согласно тому виду консонанса, который греки называют парафонией, то есть как бы звучанием рядом. Октава же, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — согласно тому виду консонанса, который называют антифонией, то есть как бы звучанием навстречу.» / EN “The intervals called the fourth and the fifth are consonant according to the kind of consonance the Greeks call paraphony, as if sounding alongside. The octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave are consonant according to the kind they call antiphony, as if sounding in answer.”
  - D · RU «Интервалы, именуемые квартой и квинтой, консонируют по тому виду консонанса, который греки называют парафонией, то есть как бы «звучанием рядом». Октава же, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по тому виду консонанса, который они называют антифонией, то есть как бы «звучанием напротив».» / EN “The intervals called the fourth and the fifth are consonant in the kind of consonance the Greeks call paraphony, meaning something like ‘sounding alongside’. The octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave are consonant in the kind they call antiphony, meaning something like ‘sounding opposite’.”
- **C** · Takes dictum as referring back to the previously mentioned fourth.
  - C · RU «Консонируют же названный интервал кварты и квинта — по тому роду консонанса, который греки называют парафонным, как бы «звучащим рядом»; а октава, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по тому роду консонанса, который называют антифонным, как бы «звучащим напротив».» / EN “The aforesaid interval of the fourth, and the fifth, are consonant according to that genus of consonance which the Greeks call paraphonon, as it were ‘sounding beside’; but the octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth and the double octave according to that genus of consonance which they call antiphonon, as it were ‘sounding opposite’.”

**Verdict** · The immediate naming pattern diatessaron dictum favours the predicative relation ‘called the fourth’. The retrospective sense is possible in context, since the fourth has already been named. In the Latin the participle modifies singular intervallum.

- **Russian** · интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта
- **English** · the interval called the fourth, and the fifth
- **Evidence** · Lewis & Short, s.v. dico (2), naming and speaking, Corpus of Latin Dictionaries [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · medium · modified

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C · doubt · sound** (modified)
  - **Printed** · diatessaron dictum interuallum, et diapente
  - **Proposal** · Консонируют же интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта… / The interval called the fourth and the fifth are consonant…
  - The singular intervallum supports the naming construction for the fourth. The retrospective rendering is still intelligible.
  - **Evidence** · Lewis & Short, s.v. dico (2), naming and speaking, Corpus of Latin Dictionaries [checked] · Xyl. 44.21–22, supplied diplomatic transcription [checked]

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Keep the contrast between Thrasyllus’ classification and the local inclusion of two compound intervals. Add a verified source before printing that parallel.
- **D** · Keep Xylander’s prefix glosses and the extension of antiphony to both octave compounds.

### X32 · Xyl. 45.2–13

*Inter paraphonum et antiphonum, hoc est assonans et obsonans, hoc differt, quod illud quidem consonat inaequali tempore, quando soni mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes aequalitate quadam leniter et concinne sibi invicem succedunt. Antiphonum autem est, cum simul eodem tempore acutum cum gravi consonat: ut cum octavus primo, undecimus quarto, duodecimus quinto, decimusquintus octavo aequaliter ascendit vel descendit in intensionibus remissionibusve, ita ut acutis graves, vel contra, respondeant proportionaliter.*

Paraphony is consonance at different times, with an even and smooth succession of notes in mutual proportions and ratios. The two accusatives in this description have no clear governor. Antiphony is simultaneous consonance of high and low, illustrated by four parallel pairs a seven-step octave apart.

- **Russian base** · B. Reads fluently, respects all pairs and gives the most intelligible account of the parallel pitch changes.
- **English base** · D. Preserves the second set of glosses, the timing and all four ordinal pairs in clear English.

**Divergence · mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes aequalitate quadam … sibi invicem succedunt**

What construction gives sense to the ungoverned accusatives?

- **A** · Supplies a bracketed participle meaning ‘keeping’; the ratios are maintained through the succession.
  - A · RU «Между парафонным и антифонным, то есть «звучащим при» и «звучащим навстречу», разница та, что первое консонирует в неравное время, когда звуки, [храня] взаимные свои пропорциональности и отношения, с некоторой равномерностью мягко и складно сменяют друг друга. Антифонное же — это когда высокое консонирует с низким вместе, в одно и то же время: например, когда восьмой [звук] с первым, одиннадцатый с четвёртым, двенадцатый с пятым, пятнадцатый с восьмым равномерно восходит или нисходит в повышениях или понижениях, так что низкие [звуки] высоким, или наоборот, отвечают пропорционально.» / EN “Between the paraphonon and the antiphonon, that is, the ‘sounding to’ and the ‘sounding over against’, the difference is this: the former is consonant at unequal time, when the notes, [keeping] their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and neatly with a certain evenness. The antiphonon, on the other hand, is when high is consonant with low together, at the same time: as when the eighth [note] rises or falls evenly with the first, the eleventh with the fourth, the twelfth with the fifth, the fifteenth with the eighth, in raisings or lowerings, so that low [notes] answer to high, or the reverse, proportionally.”
- **B, C, D** · Treat the accusatives as the respect in which the succession has evenness, broadly equivalent to an implicit ‘according to’ or ‘in’.
  - B · RU «Парафония и антифония, то есть созвучие вторящее и ответное, различаются следующим. При парафонии звуки консонируют в разное время, когда они плавно и стройно следуют друг за другом, соблюдая некоторую равномерность во взаимных пропорциях и отношениях. Антифония же бывает, когда высокий звук консонирует с низким одновременно, как при равном повышении или понижении восьмого звука с первым, одиннадцатого с четвёртым, двенадцатого с пятым, пятнадцатого с восьмым, так что низкие звуки пропорционально отвечают высоким или наоборот.» / EN “Paraphony and antiphony, that is, sounding in accompaniment and sounding in answer, differ as follows. In the former the notes are consonant at unequal times, succeeding one another smoothly and fittingly with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios. Antiphony occurs when a high note is consonant with a low one at the same time, as when the eighth and the first, the eleventh and the fourth, the twelfth and the fifth, and the fifteenth and the eighth rise or fall by equal changes in pitch, so that the low notes correspond proportionately to the high, or the reverse.”
  - C · RU «Между парафонным и антифонным, то есть «призвучным» и «противозвучным», та разница, что первое консонирует в неодинаковое время, когда звуки по своим взаимным пропорциональностям и отношениям с некоторой равномерностью мягко и слаженно сменяют друг друга. Антифонное же — когда высокое консонирует с низким вместе, в одно и то же время: например, когда восьмой [звук] с первым, одиннадцатый с четвёртым, двенадцатый с пятым, пятнадцатый с восьмым одинаково поднимается или опускается в повышениях или понижениях, так что низкие пропорционально отвечают высоким или наоборот.» / EN “Between the paraphonon and the antiphonon, that is, the ‘sounding-to’ and the ‘sounding-against’, there is this difference: the former is consonant at unequal times, when the notes, in their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and harmoniously with a certain evenness. The antiphonon is when the high is consonant with the low together, at the same time: as when the eighth [note] with the first, the eleventh with the fourth, the twelfth with the fifth, the fifteenth with the eighth rises or falls equally in raisings or lowerings, so that the low correspond proportionally to the high, or the reverse.”
  - D · RU «Парафония и антифония, то есть «звучание в ответ» и «встречное звучание», различаются следующим образом. При первой звуки консонируют в разное время: соблюдая известную равномерность в своих взаимных пропорциях и отношениях, они плавно и стройно следуют друг за другом. Антифония же возникает, когда высокий звук консонирует с низким одновременно, в один и тот же миг. Так, при повышениях или понижениях восьмой звук поднимается или опускается наравне с первым, одиннадцатый — с четвёртым, двенадцатый — с пятым, пятнадцатый — с восьмым, и низкие звуки соразмерно отвечают высоким или наоборот.» / EN “Paraphony and antiphony, that is, ‘sounding in response’ and ‘sounding opposite’, differ in this respect. In the former the notes are consonant at different times, succeeding one another smoothly and harmoniously with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios. Antiphony occurs when a high note is consonant with a low one at the very same time. Thus in raisings or lowerings [of pitch] the eighth rises or falls equally with the first, the eleventh with the fourth, the twelfth with the fifth, and the fifteenth with the eighth, so that the low notes answer the high in due proportion, or conversely.”

**Verdict** · The print gives bare accusatives with no visible participle or preposition. Sibi already supplies the complement required by succedunt in its sense of succeeding. A respect construction or bracketed ‘keeping’ gives nearly the same relation, but ordinary accusative-of-specification examples are narrower, and a lacuna remains conjectural.

- **Russian** · когда звуки с некоторой равномерностью плавно и стройно сменяют друг друга [в соответствии со] своими взаимными пропорциями и отношениями
- **English** · when the notes succeed one another smoothly and fittingly with a certain evenness [in accordance with] their mutual proportions and ratios
- **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.5–7 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. succedo II.B.2.b, Logeion [checked] · Allen and Greenough, New Latin Grammar, §370 [checked] · Allen and Greenough, New Latin Grammar, §397b [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · medium · modified · for the editor

**Divergence · inaequali tempore … simul eodem tempore**

Does Xylander contrast timing, duration, or unevenly spaced attacks?

- **A, C** · Calques unequal time, leaving unequal durations too easily available.
  - A · RU «в неравное время» / EN “at unequal time”
  - C · RU «в неодинаковое время» / EN “at unequal times”
- **B** · Russian expresses different times, while English retains unequal times.
  - B · RU «в разное время» / EN “at unequal times”
- **D** · Unambiguous non-simultaneity in both languages.
  - D · RU «в разное время» / EN “at different times”

**Verdict** · The contrast is successive versus simultaneous onset, as sibi invicem succedunt and simul eodem tempore make explicit. Neither a difference of duration nor irregular spacing is required. D makes the contrast in both languages.

- **Russian** · в разное время … одновременно
- **English** · at different times … simultaneously
- **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.3–9 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. succedo II.B.2.b, Logeion [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · added

**Errors**

- **A · en · minor language** · “the former is consonant at unequal time”. The singular wording is unidiomatic and can suggest unequal duration. **Fix** · Use ‘at different times’ in contrast with simultaneous antiphony. *(confirmed)*
- **C · ru · minor term** · “«призвучным» и «противозвучным»”. The doubted coinages replace plain descriptions in the running text. Quotation marks help distinguish the gloss, but the translator expressly identifies these forms as unestablished. **Fix** · Use «звучание рядом» or «звучание в ответ» and «встречное звучание», with the Latin terms in the note. *(confirmed)*
- **C · notes · minor note-claim** · “Mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes is an accusative of respect without a preposition”. This is one way to approximate the exposed accusatives, but does not establish a regular construction. Allen and Greenough §397b treats affected parts, chiefly with verbal or adjectival expressions capable of the specification. Sibi already supplies succedunt's ordinary dative; the printed text could also lack a governor (Allen and Greenough §§370, 397b [checked]; Lewis & Short, s.v. succedo II.B.2.b [checked]). **Fix** · Describe the accusative of respect as a possible construal and mention the unresolved alternative of an implicit or missing participle. *(added)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B · doubt · unsound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · consonat inæquali tempore
  - **Proposal** · При парафонии звуки консонируют в течение неодинаковых промежутков времени. / In paraphony the notes are consonant for unequal spans of time.
  - The contrast is between successive and simultaneous notes. Duration is possible in isolation but not the best contextual opposition.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.3–9 [checked]
- **C · doubt · unsound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · consonat inæquali tempore
  - **Proposal** · …первое консонирует в неравное по длительности время… / …the former is consonant in an unequal span of time…
  - The Xylander clause has no noun meaning span, and the explicit following simul eodem tempore controls the contrast.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.3–9 [checked]
- **D · doubt · unsound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · consonat inæquali tempore
  - **Proposal** · через неодинаковые промежутки времени / at unequal intervals of time
  - This adds unequal gaps between attacks. Successive attacks need not be irregularly spaced.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.3–9 [checked]
- **A · doubt · open** (modified · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes … sibi inuicem succedunt
  - **Proposal** · когда звуки в отношении взаимных своих пропорциональностей и отношений с некоторой ровностью мягко и слаженно сменяют друг друга / when the notes, in respect of their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and harmoniously with a certain evenness
  - The rendering can stand as an interpretation of the accusatives. The print supplies no regular governor and does not choose between a respect construction and implicit verbal material.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.5–7 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. succedo II.B.2.b, Logeion [checked]
- **D · doubt · open** (modified · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes … sibi inuicem succedunt
  - **Proposal** · звуки плавно и стройно сменяют друг друга, [следуя] своим взаимным пропорциям и отношениям с известной равномерностью / the notes succeed one another smoothly and harmoniously, [following] their mutual proportions and ratios with a certain evenness
  - The brackets correctly expose the conjectural verbal support. Nothing in the supplied scan establishes that the syntax originally included a participle.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.5–7 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. succedo II.B.2.b, Logeion [checked]
- **A · text issue · open** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes æqualitate quadam leniter et concinnè sibi inuicem succedunt
  - **Proposal** · Possible lacuna after rationes: servantes or retinentes.
  - The absence of a regular governor is genuine. The print offers no indication that exactly those words, or any words, dropped out.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.5–7 [checked] · Allen and Greenough, New Latin Grammar, §370 [checked]
- **A · doubt · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · æqualitate quadam … æqualiter ascendit uel descendit
  - **Proposal** · с неким равенством … равно восходит или нисходит / with a certain equality... rises or falls equally
  - Equality and evenness are both available. In the first clause the immediate effect is smooth succession, and in the second the two pitches change equally.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.6–11 [checked]

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · All four written pairs are separated by seven steps, so all illustrate octave parallelism. The broader class in X31 includes compound intervals but the pairings should be preserved.
- **D** · The simultaneous equal pitch change can be illustrated by multiplying both frequencies by a common factor, preserving their ratio. Keep the two Latin glosses distinct from the Greek-based labels in the note.

**Editor questions**

- Retain the suspended accusatives and mark only the supplied English and Russian link, or register a possible Latin lacuna in the apparatus?

### X33 · Xyl. 45.13–16

*Caeterum tonus, semitonium, atque diesis per se considerata, initium quidem seu principium sunt consonantiae, non tamen consonantia.*

A tone, a semitone and a diesis, taken by themselves, provide a beginning or principle of consonance while falling short of being consonances.

- **Russian base** · A = C. Preserve the two near-synonyms and the adversative force of non tamen.
- **English base** · C. Preserves both nouns and avoids treating the second denial as a temporal stage.

**Errors**

- **B · ru · minor mistranslation** · “но консонанса ещё не образуют”. «Ещё» adds the temporal nuance explicit in Vinet's nondum. Xylander's tamen is adversative. **Fix** · Use «но сами по себе не образуют консонанса» or «однако консонансом не являются». *(confirmed)*
- **D · ru · minor mistranslation** · “но ещё не консонанс”. «Ещё» adds the temporal nuance explicit in Vinet's nondum. Xylander's tamen is adversative. **Fix** · Use «но сами по себе не образуют консонанса» or «однако консонансом не являются». *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · The distinction between Vinet's nondum and Xylander's non tamen is worth keeping. Theon supplies a parallel only if its authorial text has been checked.

### X34 · Xyl. 45.16–24

*Conficitur autem diatessaron duobus ex tonis, unoque, semitonio, et praeterea quopiam exiguo: quod quia minus diesi est, appellationem peculiarem non invenit. Diapente vero ex tribus tonis, semitonioque constat. Diapason autem ex sex tonis conficitur. Diapason autem cum diatessaron, ex octo tonis et semitonio conflatur. Diapason cum diapente, ex novem tonis et semitonio. Disdiapason, ex duodecim tonis.*

The fourth has two tones, one semitone and an unnamed extra amount smaller than a diesis. The fifth has three tones and a semitone, the octave six tones, the octave with a fourth eight tones and a semitone, the octave with a fifth nine tones and a semitone, and the double octave twelve tones.

- **Russian base** · B. Preserves the additional quantity and all counts in natural Russian.
- **English base** · B = D. Both preserve the anomalous extra quantity without rewriting it and report every count accurately.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · text issue · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · duob. ex tonis, unoqúe semitonio, et præterea quo
  - **Proposal** · Remove the reading-text comma after unoque.
  - The apparent punctuated ending on the scan is the abbreviated form of -que. Unoque agrees with semitonio; the separate comma does not belong there. Et praeterea introduces an additional quantity independently of that correction.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.17 [checked]
- **C · text issue · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · duob. ex tonis, unoqúe semitonio, et præterea quo
  - **Proposal** · Remove the reading-text comma after unoque.
  - The apparent punctuated ending on the scan is the abbreviated form of -que. Unoque agrees with semitonio; the separate comma does not belong there. Et praeterea introduces an additional quantity independently of that correction.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 45.17 [checked]

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Keep the explicit distinction between a geometric half of a 9:8 tone and the 256:243 Pythagorean remainder that completes either fourth or fifth. The list’s six-tone octave exceeds 2:1 by the ratio 531441:524288.
- **D** · The unnamed positive addition in the first fourth disappears from the compound fourth count and the tetrachord enumeration in X47.

**Editor questions**

- Retain the anomalous unnamed remainder and decide how much arithmetic to show. The contradiction involving a geometric half tone is conditional on that meaning of semitone.

### X35 · Xyl. 45.24–46.3

*Proportio autem diatessaron seu quartae est sesquitertia, diapente sesquialteram constituit, diapason duplam. Diapason cum diatessaron, duplam superpartientem: diapason cum diapente, triplam.*

Xylander gives the ratios of fourth (4:3), fifth (3:2), octave (2:1), octave with fourth (8:3) and octave with fifth (3:1). The fourth is designated by both a Greek-derived name and its ordinary Latin gloss.

- **Russian base** · D. The five ratios and prescribed terminology are clear. Adjust the literal-name order if retaining the Xylander gloss.
- **English base** · A = D. Keep the gloss and all five proportional names and brackets in readable English.

**Errors**

- **B · en · minor omission** · “the interval commonly called the fourth”. The two coordinate names diatessaron seu quartae have collapsed to one. The note retains the Latin distinction, but the rough opposition between technical name and ordinary ‘fourth’ disappears in the running text. **Fix** · Use ‘the interval called “through four”, or the fourth’, or state the two Latin words explicitly in the note. *(confirmed)*
- **C · both · minor term** · “диатессарона / the diatessaron”. The Greek-derived form in the running text departs from the project’s prescribed normal term. The etymological gloss can be preserved without treating the Greek form as the main term. **Fix** · Use the normal term and put its literal value alongside or in the note. *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · The class duple superpartient includes multiple possible excesses. Here X36 specifies two thirds beyond twice the smaller term.
- **A** · The 8:3 compound was disputed. Boethius reports the Pythagorean objection in II.27 and Ptolemy’s defence in V.8–9. Cite the text of the relevant chapters when printing the note.

### X36 · Xyl. 46.3–9

*Id quo melius intelligatur, exempli causa constituatur in numeris primus terminus senarius: ad eum sesquitertius est octonarius, sesquialter novenarius, duplus duodenarius, duplus superpartiens (nimirum super bipartiens tertias) sedecim. Triplus denique, octodecim: quadruplus, viginti quatuor.*

With six as the first term, eight is sesquitertian, nine sesquialter, twelve duple, sixteen duple superpartient with an excess of two thirds of six over the double, eighteen triple and twenty-four quadruple.

- **Russian base** · B. Clear, idiomatic and arithmetically exact, with the technical parenthesis explained in its note.
- **English base** · B. Gives the six ratios and clarifies the additional two thirds without duplicating the numeric tag.

**Divergence · nimirum super bipartiens tertias**

Is this a second independent ratio, a species name, or an explanatory expansion of the preceding name?

- **A, C** · Preserves the technical superbipartient-by-thirds expression as a qualification of ‘duple’.
  - A · RU «Чтобы это лучше понять, положим для примера первым членом в числах шестёрку: сверхтретное [4:3] к ней [число] — восемь, полуторное [3:2] — девять, двукратное [2:1] — двенадцать, двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] (а именно сверхдвучастное третей) — шестнадцать; наконец, трёхкратное [3:1] — восемнадцать, четырёхкратное [4:1] — двадцать четыре.» / EN “That this may be the better understood, let the number six be set down, for example's sake, as the first term in numbers: to it the number eight is sesquitertian [4:3], nine sesquialter [3:2], twelve duple [2:1], sixteen duple superpartient [8:3] (that is to say, superbipartient thirds); lastly eighteen is triple [3:1], twenty-four quadruple [4:1].”
  - C · RU «Чтобы это лучше понять, примем для примера в числах первым членом число шесть: к нему сверхтретным [4:3] является восемь, полуторным [3:2] — девять, двукратным [2:1] — двенадцать, двукратным сверхчастным [8:3] (а именно сверхдвучастным по третям) — шестнадцать, наконец, трёхкратным [3:1] — восемнадцать, четырёхкратным [4:1] — двадцать четыре.» / EN “That this may be better understood, let the number six be set down, for example, as the first term in numbers: to it the sesquitertian [4:3] is eight, the sesquialter [3:2] nine, the duple [2:1] twelve, the duple superpartient [8:3] (namely superbipartient by thirds) sixteen; and finally the triple [3:1] eighteen, the quadruple [4:1] twenty-four.”
- **B** · Paraphrases just the excess, two thirds beyond the double.
  - B · RU «Для большей ясности возьмём в числовом примере шесть за первый член. Сверхтретное [4:3] к нему — восемь, полуторное [3:2] — девять, двукратное [2:1] — двенадцать, двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] (с избытком именно в две трети) — шестнадцать. Далее, трёхкратное [3:1] — восемнадцать, четырёхкратное [4:1] — двадцать четыре.» / EN “To make this clearer, let six be the first term in a numerical example. Eight stands to it in a sesquitertian [4:3] ratio, nine in a sesquialter [3:2], twelve in a duple [2:1], sixteen in a duple superpartient [8:3] (with precisely two thirds in excess). Finally, eighteen stands to it in a triple [3:1] ratio, and twenty-four in a quadruple [4:1].”
- **D** · Repeats the whole ratio descriptively, adding the base double and another [8:3].
  - D · RU «Чтобы это было понятнее, возьмём для примера в качестве первого члена число шесть. По отношению к нему восемь составляет сверхтретное [4:3], девять — полуторное [3:2], двенадцать — двукратное [2:1], шестнадцать — двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] (точнее, [двукратное] с избытком в две трети [8:3]). Наконец, восемнадцать составляет трёхкратное [3:1], двадцать четыре — четырёхкратное [4:1].» / EN “To make this clearer, take the number six as the first term of a numerical example. Relative to it, eight is sesquitertian [4:3], nine sesquialter [3:2], twelve duple [2:1], and sixteen duple superpartient [8:3] (specifically, [the double] plus two thirds [8:3]). Finally, eighteen is triple [3:1], and twenty-four quadruple [4:1].”

**Verdict** · The arithmetic and the printed placement fix a single ratio, 16:6 = 8:3 = 2 + 2/3. The technical parallel in Boethius confirms that the parenthesis specifies the excess. A/C preserve the lexical echo and B communicates it accurately. The second [8:3] in D follows a description, rather than another independent ratio name.

- **Russian** · двукратное сверхчастное [8:3], а именно с избытком в две трети
- **English** · duple superpartient [8:3], specifically with an excess of two thirds
- **Evidence** · Boethius, De institutione musica, ed. Friedlein (1867), I.4, Friedlein p. 192 [checked] · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 46.7–8 [checked] · Project conventions, about.md §§4.1–4.6 [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · confirmed

**Errors**

- **D · both · minor bracket** · “([the double] plus two thirds [8:3]) / ([двукратное] с избытком в две трети [8:3])”. The parenthesis explains the excess in the single just-tagged ratio. The extra numerical bracket follows a descriptive gloss and repeats a value already supplied. **Fix** · Keep [8:3] after the named duple-superpartient ratio and explain the two thirds without another numerical bracket. *(confirmed)*
- **A · both · minor language** · “superbipartient thirds / сверхдвучастное третей”. The syntactic relation of ‘thirds’ is opaque in English and the Russian genitive is unnatural. **Fix** · Either ‘superbipartient by thirds’ / «сверхдвучастное по третям» in a technical note or a plain explanation of the two-thirds excess. *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Boethius’ duplex superbipartiens gives the same structural relation, e.g. 8:3. The parenthetical expression by itself over a single base would describe 5:3, which is not the running ratio.

### X37 · Xyl. 46.9–13

*At enim scire hoc oportet, proportiones intervallorum nequaquam iudicari ad numeros sonorum vel fidium facta collatione, sed ad sonitus ipsius intensionem a gravi ad acutum.*

Interval ratios are determined by the rise of the sounding pitch from low to high. Merely comparing the numbers attached to the notes or strings, as the ordinal examples in X38 show, does not determine them.

- **Russian base** · D. Makes the reference to numbered notes or strings and the rise of pitch explicit.
- **English base** · D. Clear quantitative distinction, reinforced by the following example.

**Divergence · ad numeros sonorum vel fidium … ad sonitus ipsius intensionem a gravi ad acutum**

Does the opposition concern the count or numbering of notes, and physical tension or pitch increase?

- **A, C** · Retains the ambiguous ‘numbers’ and uses ‘tension’ for intensio.
  - A · RU «Однако следует знать вот что: о пропорциях интервалов судят отнюдь не путём сопоставления с числами звуков или струн, а по напряжению самого звучания от низкого к высокому.» / EN “But this must be known: the proportions of the intervals are by no means judged by making a comparison with the numbers of the notes or strings, but by the tension of the sound itself from low to high.”
  - C · RU «Однако надо знать, что пропорции интервалов отнюдь не определяются сопоставлением с числами звуков или струн, но [сопоставлением] с напряжением самого звучания от низкого к высокому.» / EN “But one must know this: the proportions of intervals are by no means judged by a comparison made with the numbers of notes or strings, but with the tension of the sounding itself from low to high.”
- **B** · Uses pitch rise but Russian takes numeri as the quantity of notes or strings; English ‘numbers’ leaves it less explicit.
  - B · RU «Надо, однако, знать, что пропорции интервалов определяют отнюдь не сопоставлением количества звуков или струн, а по повышению самого звучания от низкого к высокому.» / EN “One must, however, bear in mind that the proportions of intervals are assessed by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high. Comparing the numbers of notes or strings plays no part in that assessment.”
- **D** · Identifies the notes’ numbers as ordinal labels and intensio as increase in pitch.
  - D · RU «Нужно, однако, знать, что об отношениях интервалов судят вовсе не по сопоставлению чисел, обозначающих звуки или струны, а по тому, насколько само звучание повышается от низкого к высокому.» / EN “One must understand, however, that the ratios of intervals are in no way judged by a comparison of the numbers of the notes or strings, but by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high.”

**Verdict** · The calculation in X38 takes the fifth numbered note over the first, second, third and fourth (5:1, 5:2, 5:3, 5:4). It therefore uses ordinal labels. Intensio is the acoustic rise from grave to acutum. Physical string tension becomes an example later, but here sonitus and the pitch endpoints specify the rise.

- **Russian** · по числам, которыми обозначены звуки или струны, … по повышению звучания от низкого к высокому
- **English** · by the numbers assigned to notes or strings … by the rise in pitch of the sound from low to high
- **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 46.9–23 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intensio I.B and II, Logeion [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · modified

**Errors**

- **A · both · minor mistranslation** · “tension of the sound itself / напряжению самого звучания”. The running text makes the technical intensio hard to recognize as the pitch rise from low to high. A's note correctly identifies the acoustic sense and supplies a basis for revision (Lewis & Short, s.v. intensio I.B and II [checked]). **Fix** · Render ‘rise in pitch’ / «повышение звучания» here. *(modified)*
- **C · both · minor mistranslation** · “tension of the sounding itself / напряжением самого звучания”. The running text makes the technical intensio hard to recognize as the pitch rise from low to high. C's note correctly identifies the acoustic sense and supplies a basis for revision (Lewis & Short, s.v. intensio I.B and II [checked]). **Fix** · Render ‘rise in pitch’ / «повышение звучания» here. *(modified)*
- **B · ru · major mistranslation** · “количества звуков или струн”. The ratios in the next sentence compare assigned positions, not totals of notes or strings. **Fix** · Use «чисел, обозначающих звуки или струны». *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · Distinguish ordinals from the magnitudes of pitch intervals before the fifth example.

### X38 · Xyl. 46.13–21

*Nam quia intervallum diapente diximus habere sesquialteram proportionem, equidem id non intelligendum est ad excessum quantitatis respiciendo, qua in respectu se praecedentium sonorum habet. Ea namque ratione ad primum esset quincuplum, ad secundum duplum sesquialterum: at tertio collatum, eum, eiusque bessem continet: sesquiquartum vero est ad quartum, nulli autem horum omnium sesquialtera proportione quadrat.*

The 3:2 ratio of the fifth does not come from comparing the order number five with the numbers of preceding notes. Those comparisons give 5:1, 5:2, five as three plus two thirds of three, and 5:4. The printed qua clause linking the excess to preceding notes is compressed.

- **Russian base** · B. Its bracketed ordinal clarification makes the example intelligible and the four comparisons are accurate.
- **English base** · D. Makes the implicit fifth note explicit in brackets and preserves the described 5:3 without adding a ratio tag.

**Divergence · ad excessum quantitatis respiciendo, qua in respectu se praecedentium sonorum habet**

How does the qua clause attach and what quantity is compared?

- **A** · Treats the clause as a relative object in sense, approximately as if the text read quam; the interval or note ‘has’ numerical excess.
  - A · RU «Ведь раз мы сказали, что интервал квинты имеет полуторную [3:2] пропорцию, то понимать это, конечно, следует не с оглядкой на то количественное превышение, какое у него есть по отношению к предшествующим ему звукам. Ибо при таком расчёте к первому он был бы пятикратным [5:1], ко второму — двукратным полуторным [5:2]; сопоставленный же с третьим, он содержит его и ещё две его трети; к четвёртому же он сверхчетвертной [5:4]; но ни с одним из них всех он не сходится в полуторной [3:2] пропорции.» / EN “For whereas we said that the interval of the fifth has the sesquialter [3:2] proportion, this is certainly not to be understood with an eye to the excess of quantity which it has with respect to the notes preceding it. For on that reckoning it would be quintuple [5:1] to the first, duple sesquialter [5:2] to the second; while, compared with the third, it contains it and two thirds of it; and it is sesquiquartan [5:4] to the fourth; but with none of all these does it square in the sesquialter [3:2] proportion.”
- **B** · Smooths the relative construction and supplies the fifth’s ordinal number in Russian.
  - B · RU «Когда мы говорим, что у квинты полуторная [3:2] пропорция, речь, конечно, не о том, насколько её [порядковый номер] превосходит номера предшествующих звуков. При таком расчёте к первому отношение было бы пятикратным [5:1], ко второму — двукратно-полуторным [5:2]. При сопоставлении с третьим [пятый] заключает его в себе вместе с двумя его третями, а к четвёртому отношение сверхчетвертное [5:4]. Ни с одним из них полуторная [3:2] пропорция не получается.» / EN “For when we said that the fifth has a sesquialter [3:2] proportion, we certainly did not mean this in terms of its numerical excess over the notes that precede it. By that reckoning its ratio to the first would be quintuple [5:1] and to the second duple sesquialter [5:2]. Compared with the third it contains it and two thirds of it, and to the fourth it is sesquiquartan [5:4]. It stands in a sesquialter [3:2] proportion to none of them.”
- **C** · Takes qua adverbially and supplies an understood object of habet, ‘insofar as it has it’.
  - C · RU «Ведь когда мы сказали, что интервал квинты имеет полуторную [3:2] пропорцию, это, конечно, не следует понимать, глядя на избыток количества — в той мере, в какой он имеет его по отношению к предшествующим ему звукам. Ибо в таком случае по отношению к первому [звуку] он был бы пятикратным [5:1], ко второму — двукратным полуторным [5:2]; а в сравнении с третьим он содержит его и две трети его; к четвёртому же он сверхчетвертный [5:4]; ни с одним же из всех них он не согласуется в полуторной [3:2] пропорции.» / EN “For since we said that the interval of the fifth has the sesquialter [3:2] proportion, this is certainly not to be understood by looking at the excess of quantity, in so far as it has it in respect of the notes preceding it. For in that way it would be, to the first [note], quintuple [5:1]; to the second, duple sesquialter [5:2]; but compared with the third, it contains it and two thirds of it; to the fourth it is sesquiquartan [5:4]; and with none of all these does it agree in the sesquialter [3:2] proportion.”
- **D** · Keeps qua and uses context to supply the fifth note, leaving the exact Latin linkage open.
  - D · RU «Сказав, что интервал квинты имеет полуторное [3:2] отношение, мы, конечно, не имели в виду, на сколько по количеству [пятый звук] превосходит предшествующие ему звуки. Ведь при таком счёте его отношение к первому было бы пятикратным [5:1], ко второму — двукратным полуторным [5:2]. При сопоставлении с третьим пятый вмещает третий целиком и ещё две его трети. К четвёртому отношение сверхчетвертное [5:4]. Ни с одним из них полуторного [3:2] отношения не получается.» / EN “When we said that the interval of a fifth has a sesquialter [3:2] ratio, we certainly did not mean the numerical excess of [the fifth note] over the notes preceding it. On that reckoning it would be quintuple [5:1] in relation to the first, duple sesquialter [5:2] in relation to the second. Compared with the third, it contains the third and two thirds of it. It is sesquiquartan [5:4] in relation to the fourth, and it stands in a sesquialter [3:2] ratio to none of them.”

**Verdict** · The scan reads qua with no evident nasal stroke. If relative, the ablative can relate to quantitas, with se habet ‘stands’ and praecedentium ‘preceding’ used absolutely. Taking se with ‘preceding it’ favours an adverbial qua with an implicit object. The 5:1 through 5:4 progression fixes the same numerical sense. A conjectural quam could refer to quantitas, but cannot agree with masculine excessus; that would require quem.

- **Russian** · речь идёт не о численном превышении [пятого звука] над предшествующими
- **English** · this does not mean the numerical excess of [the fifth note] over the preceding notes
- **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 46.13–21 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. qua, adverbial senses [from memory]
- **Confidence and review** · medium · confirmed · for the editor

**Errors**

- **C · both · minor language** · “in so far as it has it in respect of the notes preceding it / в той мере, в какой он имеет его по отношению к предшествующим ему звукам”. The literalism produces a string of unclear pronouns with no obvious first-pass meaning. **Fix** · Use a transparent ordinal paraphrase in the text and explain the awkward qua clause in a note. *(confirmed)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · text issue · open** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · spiciendo, qua in respectu se præcedentium sonorum
  - **Proposal** · Read quam … habet, possibly on the theory of a missing abbreviation mark.
  - Quam is possible with quantitas as antecedent but cannot agree with excessus. The scan reads qua without a visible abbreviation stroke. Since the ordinal argument and plausible syntax survive with qua, emendation is unnecessary.
  - **Evidence** · Xyl. 46.15–17, supplied reading text [checked] · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 46.16 [checked]
- **C · text issue · sound** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · spiciendo, qua in respectu se præcedentium sonorum
  - **Proposal** · Keep qua, understood adverbially, with quam a possible conjecture.
  - The scan confirms qua. The retention is justified by the intelligible ordinal contrast, even though the exact attachment of the accusative se stays open.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 46.13–21 [checked]
- **C · doubt · open** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · qua in respectu se præcedentium sonorum habet
  - **Proposal** · …глядя на избыток количества, который он имеет по отношению к предшествующим ему звукам. / …by looking at the excess of quantity that it has in respect of the notes preceding it.
  - This English or Russian can convey the same arithmetic sense. As a formal rendering of a conjectural quam, it would require the antecedent quantitas to provide the feminine gender.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 46.15–17 [checked]
- **D · doubt · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · qua in respectu se præcedentium sonorum habet
  - **Proposal** · по величине того численного превосходства, какое обнаруживается при сравнении с предшествующими звуками / by the amount of the numerical excess it shows when compared with the preceding notes
  - The amount/comparison paraphrase gives the arithmetic and does not entail a change to the printed qua. An editor can retain the difficulty in a note.
  - **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 46.16–21 [checked]

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Give the excess formulas 5:2 = 2 + 1/2 and 5:3 = 1 + 2/3 and explain that bes is two thirds. The 5:3 is described in Xylander and named in Vinet. Boethius I.4 records the 5:2 type.

**Editor questions**

- Retain qua and choose whether to explain its alternatives in the commentary or refer the reader only to the ordinal argument.

### X39 · Xyl. 46.21–23

*Ergo ad intensionem acuitatis, non ad numerum sonorum respiciendum. eo enim tandem modo sesquialterum erit,*

The fifth is sesquialter at 3:2 only when assessed by the increase in pitch rather than by the notes’ number.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Render the pitch quantity clearly and preserve the ratio tag.
- **English base** · D. Reads the implicit ratio in light of the example and marks the supply.

**Divergence · ad intensionem acuitatis**

Does this mean a pitch increase or a tension of sharpness?

- **A, C** · Keeps a literal tension and acuteness or height formulation.
  - A · RU «Итак, смотреть надо на напряжение высоты, а не на число звуков: ибо лишь таким образом он и окажется полуторным [3:2],» / EN “Therefore one must look to the tension of high pitch, not to the number of the notes; for only in that way will it be sesquialter [3:2],”
  - C · RU «Следовательно, надо смотреть на напряжение высоты, а не на число звуков: ведь таким-то образом [интервал] в конце концов и будет полуторным [3:2],» / EN “Therefore one must look to the tension of acuteness, not to the number of notes; for it is in this way, in the end, that it will be sesquialter [3:2],”
- **B, D** · Means the rise in pitch.
  - B · RU «Следовательно, надо смотреть на повышение звука, а не на число звуков: только тогда отношение будет полуторным [3:2],» / EN “We must therefore consider the rise in pitch instead of the number of notes. Only in that way will the ratio be sesquialter [3:2],”
  - D · RU «Следовательно, смотреть нужно на повышение звука, а не на число звуков. Только тогда и получится полуторное [3:2] отношение,» / EN “We must therefore look to the rise in pitch, not to the number of notes. Only then will the [ratio] be sesquialter [3:2],”

**Verdict** · X37 explicitly defines intensio here from low to high, and X38 checks the 3:2 pitch change against ordinal ratios. Acuitas names high pitch or sharpness without implying a mechanical force.

- **Russian** · по повышению звучания
- **English** · by the rise in pitch
- **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 46.9–23 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intensio I.B and II, Logeion [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · modified

**Errors**

- **A · both · minor language** · “tension of high pitch / напряжение высоты”. The phrase is opaque and makes the relation of the two nouns harder to understand than the preceding definition warrants. **Fix** · Use ‘rise in pitch’ / «повышение звучания». *(confirmed)*
- **C · both · minor language** · “tension of acuteness / напряжение высоты”. The phrase is opaque and makes the relation of the two nouns harder to understand than the preceding definition warrants. **Fix** · Use ‘rise in pitch’ / «повышение звучания». *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · Record the noun acuitas and its pitch sense. The claim excluding any Classical occurrence needs independent verification before publication.

### X40 · Xyl. 46.23–24

*quod idem de reliquis existimandum est.*

The same way of judging ratios applies to the remaining intervals.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Supply the understood reference to the intervals in brackets and read on smoothly.
- **English base** · B = D. State the carry-over to the other intervals without adding a new claim.

### X41 · Xyl. 46.24–47.5

*Porro hae proportiones intervallorum, aut per differentiam longitudinis, crassitieive nervorum, aut per tensionem, quae fit conversione clavorum, deprehensae sunt, aut a ponderum tractione forte manifestius: in illis vero quae inflantur, vel per cavitatis amplitudinem, vel ab intensione et remissione spiritus.*

The ratios were found through differences in string length or thickness, through tension adjusted by turning pegs, or perhaps more evidently through the pull of weights. For blown instruments the variables are cavity size and the strengthening or weakening of breath.

- **Russian base** · D. Natural Russian distinguishes mechanical string tension from an increase or decrease in the breath.
- **English base** · B = D. Both retain the set of physical variables and render the force of blowing clearly.

**Divergence · per cavitatis amplitudinem … ab intensione et remissione spiritus**

Is Xylander specifying bore width or cavity size more generally, and what changes in the breath?

- **A** · General cavity size; the breath is tensed or relaxed.
  - A · RU «Далее, эти пропорции интервалов были обнаружены либо по различию длины или толщины струн, либо по натяжению, производимому поворотом колков, либо — пожалуй, нагляднее — по тяге грузов; а в тех [инструментах], в которые дуют, — либо по величине полости, либо по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания.» / EN “Further, these proportions of the intervals were detected either through the difference in the length or thickness of the strings, or through the stretching that is produced by the turning of the pegs, or, perhaps more plainly, from the pull of weights; and in those [instruments] that are blown, either through the size of the cavity, or from the tension and relaxation of the breath.”
- **B** · General cavity size; strength of breath increases or decreases.
  - B · RU «Эти пропорции интервалов обнаружили по различию в длине или толщине струн, по натяжению, которое создаётся поворотом колков, либо, пожалуй, нагляднее — по тяге грузов. В духовых инструментах их обнаружили по величине полости либо по усилению и ослаблению дыхания.» / EN “These proportions of intervals were discovered from differences in the length or thickness of strings, or from the tension produced by turning the pegs, or perhaps more plainly from the pull of weights. In instruments that are blown, they were discovered from the size of the cavity or from the strengthening and weakening of the breath.”
- **C** · Specifically cavity or bore width; breath is tensed or slackened.
  - C · RU «Далее, эти пропорции интервалов были обнаружены либо через различие длины или толщины струн, либо через натяжение, которое производится поворачиванием колков, либо — пожалуй, нагляднее — по тяге грузов; а в тех [инструментах], в которые дуют, — либо через ширину полости, либо по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания.» / EN “Further, these proportions of intervals were discovered either through difference in the length or thickness of the strings, or through the tightening produced by turning the pegs, or, perhaps more clearly, from the pull of weights; and in those [instruments] that are blown, either through the width of the bore or from the tension and slackening of the breath.”
- **D** · General cavity size; strengthening or slackening of the flow of air.
  - D · RU «Эти отношения интервалов обнаружили по различию в длине или толщине струн, по их натяжению при повороте колков или, быть может, ещё нагляднее, по натяжению под действием грузов. Для духовых [инструментов] их установили либо по величине внутренней полости, либо по усилению и ослаблению струи воздуха.» / EN “These interval ratios were discovered from differences in the length or thickness of strings, or from the tension produced by turning the pegs, or perhaps more clearly from the pull of weights. In wind [instruments] they were discovered either from the size of the cavity or from the strengthening and slackening of the breath.”

**Verdict** · Amplitudo gives the magnitude or extent of the cavity without specifying a width as against another dimension. Spiritus here belongs to the blown instrument and the pair describes increasing and decreasing the effort or strength of breath, in contrast to string tensio. Theon explicitly mentions the breadth of cavities. The parallel explains the narrower possibility, while Xylander’s Latin word also permits the size of a cavity in general.

- **Russian** · по величине полости или по усилению и ослаблению струи воздуха
- **English** · by the size of the cavity or by strengthening and weakening the breath
- **Evidence** · Xyl. 46.24–47.5, supplied reading and diplomatic text [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica, ed. Friedlein (1867), I.11 [checked] · Theon of Smyrna, Expositio, ed. Hiller pp. 56–57, specifically p. 57 lines 1–7 [checked] <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenGreekAndLatin/First1KGreek/master/data/tlg1724/tlg001/tlg1724.tlg001.1st1K-grc1.xml#L1944-L1951>
- **Confidence and review** · high · modified

**Errors**

- **C · both · minor mistranslation** · “width of the bore / ширину полости”. Specifying transverse width narrows the unspecific magnitude in amplitudo cavitatis. **Fix** · Use ‘size of the cavity’ / «величина полости». *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · Distinguish clavi as tuning pegs, string tensio and the quite different intensio and remissio of breath.
- **D** · For a string of fixed length and linear density, a 4:1 ratio of applied tensions gives an octave. Keep this as modern physical clarification beside the ancient report, which supplies no individual weights.

### X42 · Xyl. 47.5–17

*Porro systematum aliud est tetrachordum, quod quatuor componitur nervis, omnem vim intervalli diatessaron complectens: aliud pentachordum, quinque ex nervis constitutum, diapente intervalli omnem potestatem in se continens: aliud octachordum, octo ex nervis constructum, omnem octavae materiem ambiens. Est et quod continentibus sedecim nervis conficitur, omnem disdiapason (decimamquintam hodie vocant) intervalli ambitum comprehendens, eoque ad duplices usus habile, quod et praedicta intervalla, et ex his ipsis composita complectitur.*

A tetrachord of four strings embraces the fourth, a pentachord of five the fifth and an octachord of eight the octave. A further system of sixteen consecutive strings encompasses the double octave, called the fifteenth in contemporary usage. It comprises both the named intervals and combinations of them.

- **Russian base** · D. Clear structure, and «квинтдецима» makes the Renaissance interval name comprehensible without changing the string count.
- **English base** · D. Readable while retaining the four descriptions and the conflicting sixteen and fifteenth.

**Errors**

- **A · notes · minor note-claim** · “the two octachords are counted separately”. X15 expressly describes a sixteen-string structure on fifteen physical strings and reuses the last note as the beginning of the next series. X42 instead expressly says sixteen consecutive strings. The shared boundary offers a possible explanation, but the latter passage gives no separate count of two component octachords (Xylander 41.25–42.5 and 47.12–16 [checked]). **Fix** · Give the two counts as written. Mention shared-endpoint counting as a possibility for the sixteen in this passage. *(modified)*
- **C · notes · minor note-claim** · “as in X15, the shared note is counted twice”. X15 expressly describes a sixteen-string structure on fifteen physical strings and reuses the last note as the beginning of the next series. X42 instead expressly says sixteen consecutive strings. The shared boundary offers a possible explanation, but the latter passage gives no separate count of two component octachords (Xylander 41.25–42.5 and 47.12–16 [checked]). **Fix** · Give the two counts as written. Mention shared-endpoint counting as a possibility for the sixteen in this passage. *(modified)*
- **B · both · minor sign** · “Есть и система из шестнадцати следующих подряд струн. / There is also a system made up of sixteen consecutive strings.”. The implied word system is added unmarked in both languages. Quod is substantival, and a pronoun can represent it directly. **Fix** · Use «Есть и такая, что…» / ‘There is also one that…’ or put the supplied noun in brackets. *(modified)*
- **D · both · minor sign** · “Есть и система из шестнадцати струн / There is also a system made up of sixteen consecutive strings.”. The antecedent of the substantival quod is allowed to stand without the repeated noun. The project requires brackets when the noun is supplied explicitly for clarity. **Fix** · Use «Есть и такая, что…» / ‘There is also one that…’ or bracket the repeated noun. *(added)*

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · The double octave spans fourteen steps and fifteen distinct note positions. Here sixteen strings is stated alongside the contemporary name ‘fifteenth’. Counting the same common endpoint twice is one possible explanation.

**Editor questions**

- Should the note connect the sixteen strings conjecturally to two separate octachord counts or leave the discrepancy unexplained?

### X43 · Xyl. 47.17–21

*Inter systemata proinde, ac in medio eorum concepta intervalla hoc interest, quod systematum omnis constitutio in materia spectatur: cogitatione autem separata materia, intervalla intelliguntur.*

The constitution of a system is considered in matter. Its contained intervals are grasped by mentally putting matter aside.

- **Russian base** · B. Concise distinction between the contained intervals and the mental act of abstracting from matter.
- **English base** · B. Uses ‘comprised within’ to keep the mental abstraction in its proper clause.

**Divergence · in medio eorum concepta intervalla**

Does concepta mean contained or conceived in thought?

- **A, B, C** · The intervals comprised physically or structurally within systems.
  - A · RU «Между системами, стало быть, и интервалами, заключёнными внутри них, различие то, что всякое устроение систем усматривается в материи, интервалы же мыслятся, когда материя отделена мыслью.» / EN “Between systems, accordingly, and the intervals contained in their midst there is this difference: every constitution of systems is viewed in matter, whereas intervals are understood when matter has been set apart in thought.”
  - B · RU «Итак, системы отличаются от заключённых в них интервалов тем, что всё устройство системы рассматривается в материи, а интервалы постигаются в отвлечении от материи.» / EN “Systems accordingly differ from the intervals comprised within them in that the whole constitution of a system is observed in matter, whereas intervals are understood by setting matter aside in thought.”
  - C · RU «Итак, между системами и интервалами, заключёнными внутри них, та разница, что всякое устроение систем рассматривается в материи, а интервалы постигаются, когда материя отделена мыслью.» / EN “Between systems, then, and the intervals comprised in their midst there is this difference: every constitution of systems is viewed in matter, while intervals are understood when matter has been separated off in thought.”
- **D** · The intervals as mentally conceived within them.
  - D · RU «Различие между системами и интервалами, которые мы мыслим внутри них, в следующем. Строение системы во всей его полноте рассматривают в материи, а интервалы постигают, мысленно отвлекаясь от материи.» / EN “Systems differ from the intervals conceived within them in this respect. The whole structure of a system is considered in matter, whereas intervals are grasped by abstracting from matter in thought.”

**Verdict** · Concipere can describe containing or conceiving. In medio eorum gives the containing structure, whereas Xylander separately marks abstraction with cogitatione … separata materia. The contained sense best preserves that contrast.

- **Russian** · интервалы, заключённые внутри систем
- **English** · the intervals contained within the systems
- **Evidence** · Lewis & Short, s.v. concipio, with spatial and mental uses [from memory] · Xyl. 47.17–21, supplied diplomatic text [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · confirmed

**Errors**

- **D · both · minor construal** · “intervals conceived within them / интервалами, которые мы мыслим внутри них”. In the opening comparison the intervals are specified by their structural location. The later clause states how the mind can grasp them separately. **Fix** · Use ‘the intervals contained within them’ and distinguish the later mental act. *(confirmed)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C · doubt · unsound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · in medio eorum concepta interualla
  - **Proposal** · …между системами и интервалами, мыслимыми внутри них… / …between systems and the intervals conceived within them…
  - A possible lexical shade in isolation, but the location phrase and explicit second-clause abstraction favour ‘contained’.
  - **Evidence** · Xyl. 47.17–21, supplied diplomatic text [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. concipio [from memory]

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · Matter means the concrete realization in strings or notes. The interval is intelligible in abstraction from that realization.

### X44 · Xyl. 47.21–48.1

*Harmoniae proprium est, ut ea systemata construat atque concinnet ad horum melodiae (eam cantus seu cantilenae modulationem fere dixeris) generum aliquod, quae sunt diatonon, chromaticum, et harmonicum, cui ab ipsa Harmonia nomen obtigit.*

Harmony builds and fits its systems to one of three genera of melody. Melody can be termed the measured shaping of song or tune. The genera are diatonic, chromatic and harmonic, with the last taking its name from harmony itself.

- **Russian base** · B. Clear syntax and an intelligible parenthetical definition of melodia.
- **English base** · B = D. Both preserve the three genera, the application to systems and the parenthetical gloss without suggesting a key change.

**Divergence · eam cantus seu cantilenae modulationem fere dixeris**

What modern musical sense does modulatio carry in the gloss on melody?

- **A** · Measured shaping of song or a tune.
  - A · RU «Гармонии свойственно строить и слаживать эти системы сообразно какому-либо из таких родов мелодии (её можно бы приблизительно назвать мерным ведением пения, или напева): диатонического, хроматического и гармонического, получившего имя от самой гармонии.» / EN “It is proper to harmony to construct and frame those systems according to some one of these genera of melody (you might roughly call it the measured shaping of song, or of a tune), which are the diatonic, the chromatic, and the harmonic, to which the name has fallen from Harmony itself.”
- **B, D** · Melodic movement or shaping of singing or a tune.
  - B · RU «Гармонии свойственно строить и стройно сочетать эти системы сообразно одному из родов мелодии (её можно приблизительно определить как мелодическое движение пения или напева). Это диатонический, хроматический и гармонический роды. Последний получил имя от самой гармонии.» / EN “It belongs to harmony to construct and arrange these systems according to one of the genera of melody (which one might broadly call the melodic movement of singing or of a tune). These are the diatonic, chromatic, and harmonic genera. The last takes its name from harmony itself.”
  - D · RU «Гармонии свойственно строить эти системы и согласовывать их с одним из следующих родов мелодии (мелодией, пожалуй, можно назвать мелодический строй пения или напева). Эти роды — диатонический, хроматический и гармонический, получивший имя от самой гармонии.» / EN “It belongs to harmony to construct these systems and fit them to one of the following genera of melody (one might perhaps describe melody as the melodic shaping of singing or of a tune). The genera are the diatonic, the chromatic and the harmonic, which takes its name from harmony itself.”
- **C** · In Russian melodic movement. In English uses ‘modulation’, intending a classical sense explained in the note.
  - C · RU «Свойство гармонии — строить и слаживать эти системы применительно к какому-либо из этих родов мелодии (её, пожалуй, можно было бы назвать мелодическим движением пения или напева), каковы диатонический, хроматический и гармонический, которому имя досталось от самой Гармонии.» / EN “It is proper to harmony to construct and attune those systems for some one of these genera of melody (you might almost call it the modulation of song or tune), which are the diatonic, the chromatic and the harmonic, to which [last] the name fell from Harmony itself.”

**Verdict** · The lexical sense is regulated or measured succession in singing or in a tune. A/B/D are usable descriptions. Modern English ‘modulation’ ordinarily implies a change of key, which is foreign to the definition of melodia.

- **Russian** · её можно было бы назвать размеренным движением пения или напева
- **English** · one might describe it as the measured shaping of singing or of a tune
- **Evidence** · Lewis & Short, s.v. modulor, use of singing and measured voice, Scaife ATLAS [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. modulatio, rhythmical measure and singing, Corpus of Latin Dictionaries [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica, ed. Friedlein (1867), I.21 [checked] · Project conventions, about.md §§4.1–4.6 [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · modified

**Errors**

- **C · en · minor term** · “the modulation of song or tune”. In modern musical prose ‘modulation’ implies a key change unless explicitly qualified. The note explains the different sense, but the running term should already convey it. **Fix** · Use ‘measured movement’ or ‘melodic shaping’ in the running text. *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Keep Xylander’s alternation between diatonon here and diatonicum elsewhere. The project's harmonic denotes the genus conventionally called enharmonic, and Xylander explicitly connects its name with harmony.

### X45 · Xyl. 48.1–7

*Nam siquidem harmonia ipsa seu consonantiarum coaptatio potissimum per tonos progrediatur, eo modo diatonicum conficitur genus melodiae: sin per semitonia, chromaticum, quasi coloratum dicas: sin ipsa harmonia diesibus maiore sui parte absolvatur, harmonicus eo modo cantus conflatur.*

Predominantly tonal motion produces the diatonic genus. Motion by semitones produces the chromatic, literally as if coloured. Harmony realized for the most part by dieses produces harmonic singing.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Both provide natural descriptions and keep the colour gloss.
- **English base** · B. Clear conditional structure and accurate rendering of the three genera and of the gloss.

**Errors**

- **B · notes · minor note-claim** · “Diatonon and chromaticum are the names of the diatonic and chromatic genera.”. The statement correctly describes X44, but in this passage the adduced Latin form is diatonicum, not diatonon. **Fix** · Identify the X44 form as a variant and give diatonicum for X45. *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · Read predominance as the number of smaller steps of the characteristic type in each tetrachord. This explains the two dieses of X51 despite the greater total width of the ditone.

### X46 · Xyl. 48.7–10

*Neque tamen aut solis ex tonis universa harmonia componitur, aut ex solis semitoniis, solisve diesibus: id enim fieri nequit.*

Harmony as a whole cannot consist exclusively of tones, exclusively of semitones, or exclusively of dieses.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Both preserve the three independent exclusions in natural Russian.
- **English base** · D. Keeps the assertion followed by the grounds in a second sentence.

### X47 · Xyl. 48.10–14

*Sed quoniam quidem tribus ex intervallis constat diatessaron, comprehensum videlicet duobus tonis et semitonio: in harmoniam coaptatis duobus tonis, opus est reliquum semitonio perfici.*

The fourth here has three steps, named as two tones and a semitone. With the two tones fitted into the harmony, the remaining part must be completed with a semitone.

- **Russian base** · B. Gives the three steps and brackets its supplied noun.
- **English base** · B. Clear syntax and accurate step count.

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · With a Pythagorean fourth and the 9:8 tone, the remainder after two tones is 256:243. Point out that X47 itself has no further increment of the kind named at X34.

### X48 · Xyl. 48.14–16

*hocque genus diatessaron, diatonicum vocatur: quanquam ad eius constitutionem, praeter duos tonos, semitonium quoque accesserit.*

This arrangement of the fourth is called diatonic even though a semitone enters the structure in addition to the two tones.

- **Russian base** · D. Natural and exact.
- **English base** · D. Keeps the concession and makes clear which notes enter the genus.

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Explain the concession by the etymological association of diatonic with proceeding through tones, with Boethius I.21 as a parallel.

### X49 · Xyl. 48.16–18

*Iam si duo sint semitonia, quod superest, ex coalitu utique conflatur intervallum, trisemitonium incompositum:*

When two of the intervals are semitones, the remaining span forms one incomposite trihemitone by coalescence.

- **Russian base** · B. Clear sequence and idiomatic word order.
- **English base** · D. Keeps utique and describes the single resulting interval precisely.

**Errors**

- **A · both · minor language** · “fused by coalescence / сплавляется, через слияние”. The literal verbal doubling is ungainly, especially the Russian inserted commas. It can be preserved better by choosing complementary words. **Fix** · Use ‘is formed by coalescence’ / «образуется путём слияния». *(confirmed)*

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · A span equivalent to three semitones sounds as one step, so it is incomposite in this context. Boethius I.23 explains the same distinction.

### X50 · Xyl. 48.19–22

*atque ita ex duobus semitoniis et trisemitonio confundetur genus diatessaron, quod Chromaticum vocatur, ad movendos affectus et luctum ciendum efficacius.*

Combining two semitones with the trihemitone produces the chromatic genus of the fourth. Xylander describes it as the more effective for stirring the feelings and arousing grief.

- **Russian base** · B. Natural collocation and retains the two effects.
- **English base** · A. Keeps the future of confundetur and the causal link to X49 in readable English.

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Keep the separate ethical or emotional characterization and contrast with Vinet's more specifically mournful description.
- **D** · The supplied emendation diatessaron for printed diatesseron is confirmed by the repeated spelling on p. 48.

### X51 · Xyl. 48.22–49.1

*Sin melodia per duas dieses procedat, alterum intervallorum erit ditonus ex coalitu: idque genus diatessaron, Harmonicum dicetur, quod eo quia sit optimum, a communi harmoniae vocabulo nomen invenit.*

If melody moves through two dieses, the other or remaining interval is a ditone formed by coalescence. This genus of fourth is called harmonic, a name derived from the general word harmony because the genus is considered best.

- **Russian base** · B. Keeps the odd word ‘other’ and provides the contextual solution in a note.
- **English base** · B. Preserves the written expression and specifies the inferred remaining interval separately.

**Divergence · alterum intervallorum erit ditonus ex coalitu**

What pair, if any, does ‘the other’ imply?

- **A, B** · Preserves ‘the other of the intervals’ with the contextual inference that it is the single remaining step.
  - A · RU «Если же мелодия идёт по двум диесам, то другой из интервалов будет дитоном, [возникшим] через слияние; и этот род кварты будет называться гармоническим: оттого, что он наилучший, он и получил имя от общего слова «гармония».» / EN “But if the melody proceeds through two dieses, the other of the intervals will be a ditone by coalescence; and that genus of the fourth will be called harmonic, which, for the reason that it is the best, has found its name from the common word ‘harmony’.”
  - B · RU «Если же мелодия движется по двум диесам, то другой из интервалов будет дитоном, образованным слиянием. Этот род кварты назовут гармоническим. Поскольку он наилучший, он получил имя от общего слова «гармония».» / EN “If melody proceeds by two dieses, the other of the intervals will be a ditone formed by coalescence. This genus of the fourth will be called harmonic. Because it is the finest, it has taken its name from the general term “harmony.””
- **C** · Interprets ‘other’ as the second of two kinds of interval, diesis and ditone, in the note.
  - C · RU «Если же мелодия продвигается двумя диесами, то другим из интервалов путём срастания будет дитон; и этот род кварты будет называться гармоническим: поскольку он наилучший, он получил имя от общего слова «гармония».» / EN “But if the melody proceeds by two dieses, the other of the intervals will be a ditone, by coalescence; and this genus of the fourth will be called harmonic, which, because it is the best, took its name from the common word ‘harmony’.”
- **D** · Uses a less marked ‘the other interval’ and explains it as the third, remaining step.
  - D · RU «Если же мелодия движется по двум диесам, то другим интервалом будет дитон, возникший из слияния. Этот род кварты называют гармоническим. Из всех [родов] он наилучший и потому назван общим именем гармонии.» / EN “If the melody proceeds by two dieses, the other interval will be a ditone formed by fusion. This genus of the fourth will be called harmonic. Being the best, it has taken its name from the general term for harmony.”

**Verdict** · The scan reads alterum. Besides its usual opposition between two things, alter can denote a further member of a class. The tetrad's three intervals (X47) already include two dieses, so the remaining step has to be the ditone. C's two kinds are a possible explanation of the usual binary value, but no word for kinds stands here. In the running text, the other interval preserves the looseness, and the note can specify that it is the remaining step.

- **Russian** · другим интервалом будет дитон, образованный слиянием
- **English** · the other interval will be a ditone formed by coalescence
- **Evidence** · Xylander, Basel (1556), supplied scan, p. 48.22–25 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica, ed. Friedlein (1867), I.21 and I.23 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. alter I.A and II.A, Logeion [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · medium · modified · for the editor

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B · text issue · sound** (modified · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · alterum interuallorum
  - **Proposal** · Retain alterum and understand the interval left after the two dieses. / Если же мелодия движется по двум диесам, то оставшийся интервал будет дитоном, образованным слиянием. / If melody proceeds by two dieses, the remaining interval will be a ditone formed by coalescence.

**Notes to keep**

### X52 · Xyl. 49.1–3

*Neque alia ratione etiam ab harmonia reliqua systematum genera conficiuntur.*

Harmony forms the remaining genera of systems in the same way.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Makes the implicit same-method conclusion clear in Russian.
- **English base** · B = D. Idiomatic active English that preserves harmony as the agent.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · unsound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · Neque alia ratione etiam ab harmonia reliqua systematum genera conficiuntur
  - **Proposal** · out of harmony / из гармонии
  - Harmony already constructs and arranges the systems in X44. The passive with ab here assigns the same agent, whereas the material is the component intervals.
