### V31 · Vinet 46.1–5

*Horum autem adauctu, alia quoque simplicia intervalla fiunt, Tridiesis, Trihemitonium, Ditonium, et Tritonium; et ut volueris, et rythmus tulerit, quomodo ex sequentibus cognoscetur.*

Increasing the preceding intervals gives other simple intervals, including tridiesis, trihemitone, ditone and tritone, with further choices according to one's wishes and what rhythm permits.

- **Russian base** · D. The bracketed supplemental word closes the ellipsis and retains the terminology of simple intervals.
- **English base** · D. Clearly presents the extension of the list and marks the supplement.

**Divergence · alia quoque simplicia intervalla … et ut volueris, et rythmus tulerit**

What does the elliptical continuation allow?

- **A** · Preserves the clause suspended after the last named interval.
  - A · RU «Приращением же этих [интервалов] образуются и другие простые интервалы: тридиеса, трёхполутон, дитон и тритон — и как тебе будет угодно и как допустит ритм, что станет ясно из дальнейшего.» / EN “By the increase of these, moreover, other simple intervals also arise: the tridiesis, the trihemitone, the ditone and the tritone; and as you wish and as the rhythm allows, as will be learned from what follows.”
- **B** · Expands it into a fresh sentence allowing the procedure to continue.
  - B · RU «При увеличении этих [интервалов] получаются и другие несоставные интервалы: тридиеса, трёхполутон, дитон и тритон. Продолжать можно как угодно, насколько позволяет ритм, как станет ясно из дальнейшего.» / EN “By augmenting these [intervals], other incomposite intervals arise: the tridiesis, trihemitone, ditone, and tritone. You may proceed as you wish and as rhythm permits, as will be seen from what follows.”
- **C, D** · Extends the series with bracketed further intervals as choice and rhythm permit.
  - C · RU «Приращением же их образуются и другие простые интервалы — тридиеса, трёхполутон, дитон и тритон; и [иные] — как будет угодно и как позволит ритм, что станет понятно из последующего.» / EN “By the increase of these, other simple intervals too are formed: the tridiesis, the trihemitone, the ditone and the tritone; and [others], as you will and as the rhythm allows, as will be learned from what follows.”
  - D · RU «С увеличением этих [интервалов] получаются и другие простые интервалы: тридиеса, трёхполутон, дитон, тритон и [другие] — какие пожелаешь и какие допустит ритм, как станет ясно из дальнейшего.» / EN “By increasing these [intervals] one obtains other simple intervals too, the tridiesis, the trihemitone, the ditone and the tritone, and [others] as one wishes and as rhythm will allow, as will be clear from what follows.”

**Verdict** · The antecedent alia intervalla makes the extension of the list plausible and permits the brief bracketed supplement. The clause can also be read as a manner qualification of the continuing process. Fero with an abstract subject licenses permits. Vinet's own III.4 calls these simple and explains the word in V32.

- **Russian** · …тритон и [другие] — по желанию и как позволит ритм
- **English** · …the tritone and [others], as one wishes and as rhythm permits
- **Evidence** · Tournon 1591, p. 46, supplied page image [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. fero, technical and figurative intransitive senses, Scaife ATLAS [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · medium · modified · for the editor

**Errors**

- **B · both · minor sign** · “Продолжать можно как угодно / You may proceed as you wish”. The construction supplies a new action and sentence that the Latin leaves elliptical. Its meaning is plausible, but the supplementation should be marked or kept brief. **Fix** · и [другие] — по желанию / and [others], as one wishes *(confirmed)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C · doubt · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · & vt volueris, & rythmus tulerit
  - **Proposal** · and [they are formed] as you will and as the rhythm allows
  - The ut clause can modify an understood repetition of fiunt. The list and following discussion favor carrying the permission forward to other intervals.
  - **Evidence** · Tournon 1591, p. 46, supplied page image [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. fero, technical and figurative intransitive senses, Scaife ATLAS [checked]
- **A · doubt · open** (added · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · Horum autem adauctu, alia quoque simpli / cia interualla fiunt, Tridíesis, Trihemito- / nium, Ditonium, et Tritonium; et vt volueris, et / rythmus tulerit, quomodo ex sequentibus / cognoscetur. Simplicia autem ideo dicta
  - **Proposal** · Read the clause as permitting the listed intervals to be used as one wishes.
  - The adverbial clause can limit the use or forming of intervals. Only forming is expressed by fiunt, and the open list with alia quoque favors further possible intervals.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 46, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short s.v. fero [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=fero>

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · Retain the contrast between Vinet's simplicia, followed by its explanation, and Xylander's compositae in the parallel list and incomposita in the explanation. Identify the forms trihemitonium, ditonium and tritonium (Vinet V31–V32 and Xylander X26–X27 [checked]).

**Editor questions**

- Check whether rythmus should be annotated here. The wording gives no separate explanation of the rhythmic constraint.
- Decide whether to supply “other intervals” in brackets or leave the scope of the final clauses open.

### V32 · Vinet 46.5–10

*Simplicia autem ideo dicta sunt, quod veluti unum ex coniunctis confusisque tribus diesibus, seu tribus hemitoniis seu multis etiam tonis conficiatur melos, quod licet multorum vim habeat intervallorum, tamen per unicum resonet intervallum.*

The intervals are called simple because the combined smaller intervals form, as it were, a single melodic strain. It has the force of many intervals but is sounded through just one interval.

- **Russian base** · D. Напев is a recognizable rendering of the Greek loan. Adjust звучит как to make clear it actually sounds through one interval.
- **English base** · D. Melodic strain is appropriately limited in scale, and through just one interval preserves the demonstrative contrast.

**Divergence · veluti unum … conficiatur melos**

Does melos here require a technical loan, or can it be rendered as melody or strain?

- **A, C** · The running text keeps the loan melos, and the notes narrow it to one heard step.
  - A · RU «Простыми же они названы потому, что из трёх диес, или трёх полутонов, или даже многих тонов, соединённых и слитых, образуется как бы единый мелос, который, хотя и имеет силу многих интервалов, звучит всё же одним-единственным интервалом.» / EN “They are called simple because out of three dieses, or three semitones, or even many tones, joined and blended together, there is made as it were a single melos, which, although it has the force of many intervals, nevertheless sounds through one single interval.”
  - C · RU «Простыми же они названы потому, что из соединённых и слитых воедино трёх диес, или трёх полутонов, или даже многих тонов составляется как бы один мелос, который, хотя и имеет силу многих интервалов, звучит, однако, через один-единственный интервал.» / EN “They are called simple because, as it were, a single melos is made from three dieses joined and fused together, or from three semitones, or even from many tones, a melos which, though it has the force of many intervals, nevertheless sounds through a single interval.”
- **B** · Melody, with an explanatory note about a single interval.
  - B · RU «Несоставными же они зовутся потому, что из трёх соединённых и слившихся диес, или трёх полутонов, или даже многих тонов образуется как бы единая мелодия. Хотя она заключает в себе силу многих интервалов, звучит она в пределах одного-единственного интервала.» / EN “They are called incomposite because, from three dieses joined and merged, or three semitones, or even many tones, what is virtually one melody is formed. Although it has the force of many intervals, it sounds through a single interval.”
- **D** · Melodic strain or напев, a short musical utterance.
  - D · RU «Простыми же они названы потому, что из трёх соединённых и слившихся диес, или из трёх полутонов, или даже из многих тонов слагается как бы один напев, который, хотя и заключает в себе силу многих интервалов, всё же звучит как один-единственный интервал.» / EN “They are called simple because three dieses joined and blended together, or three semitones, or even many tones, form as it were a single melodic strain which, although it has the force of many intervals, sounds through just one interval.”

**Verdict** · Melos has common musical senses from tune to strain. In this sentence the melodic utterance traverses only one interval, so a brief strain or melodic movement is safer than the unglossed loan in running text. Veluti qualifies the unity of the melodic strain. Per unicum states that the sounded interval is one.

- **Russian** · образуется как бы единый напев, который … звучит в одном-единственном интервале
- **English** · what is formed is, as it were, a single melodic strain, which … sounds through just one interval
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 46, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. melos I [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=melos> · Boethius, De institutione musica I.23, an incomposite interval sounded in one step [checked] <https://la.wikisource.org/wiki/De_musica_(Boethius,_ed._Friedlein)/Liber_primus> · about.md §4.1 [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · medium · modified

**Errors**

- **D · ru · minor construal** · “всё же звучит как один-единственный интервал”. The melodic strain sounds through one interval in the author's explanation. Как can make the single interval a mere likeness, and it is the unity of melos that is qualified by veluti. **Fix** · всё же звучит в одном-единственном интервале *(confirmed)*
- **C · notes · minor note-claim** · ““many tones” must cover the ditone (2) and the tritone (3) of V31”. The previous examples illustrate a possible reference, but multis tonis leaves the number of tones unspecified and V31 allows further formations. **Fix** · Give the ditone and tritone as possible illustrations and keep the plural indefinite. *(added)*

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · Keep the practical explanation that a single performed step may have the size of several units, without performing separate intermediate notes. Record Latin melos against Xylander's cantilena in a note (Vinet V32 and Xylander X27 [checked]).

*Caeterum alia praeter illa sunt intervalla, Dia-tessaron, Dia-pente, et Dia-pason nuncupata:*

There are other intervals in addition to the preceding ones, named the fourth, fifth and octave. The sentence continues with their distinguishing feature in V34.

- **Russian base** · A = C. Both use the project terms and leave the continuation syntactically possible.
- **English base** · A = C. Both preserve the naming distinction and the continuation.

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · Record the hyphenated forms and give the literal elements ‘through four,’ ‘through five’ and ‘through all’. Explain the count with V34–V35 (about.md §§3, 4.2 and Tournon 1591, p. 46, supplied page image [checked]).

### V34 · Vinet 46.13–47.7

*sed haec non sine medio, neque non-composita sunt, quando per media omnia supradicta intervalla incedunt: quae ratione quidem vocis primi soni ad ultimum, intervalla appellantur: sed a numero tamen ordineque, qui in medio sunt, sonorum, haec nomina receperunt: Dia-tessaron, ut quod per quatuor: Dia-pente, per quinque: Dia-pason per octo sonos aut chordas ingrediatur termineturque.*

The fourth, fifth and octave have intervening steps and are composite. Their status as intervals depends on how the first note sounds in relation to the last, and their individual names on the ordered count of notes or strings spanned, four, five and eight respectively.

- **Russian base** · D. Clear about the intervening steps and the comparison of the endpoints.
- **English base** · D. Preserves the relation between endpoints and avoids implying an unstated numerical calculation.

**Divergence · ratione quidem vocis primi soni ad ultimum**

Does ratio specify a numerical ratio, a relation of endpoints, or a sounding from one endpoint to the other?

- **A, C** · A ratio between the first and last sounding pitches in English, with the Russian also allowing a broader relation.
  - A · RU «но они не без середины и не являются несоставными, поскольку проходят через все названные выше промежуточные интервалы; интервалами они называются по отношению голоса первого звука к последнему, однако имена эти получили от числа и порядка звуков, находящихся в середине: кварта — как идущая и завершающаяся через четыре, квинта — через пять, октава — через восемь звуков или струн.» / EN “but these are not without a middle, nor are they incomposite, since they advance through all the aforesaid intervals lying between; they are called intervals by virtue of the ratio of the voice of the first note to the last, yet they have received these names from the number and order of the notes that lie between: the fourth, as that which proceeds and terminates through four; the fifth, through five; the octave, through eight notes or strings.”
  - C · RU «но они не лишены середины и не являются несоставными, поскольку проходят через все вышеназванные интервалы как через промежуточные; интервалами они называются по отношению голоса первого звука к последнему, а названия свои получили по числу и порядку звуков, находящихся в середине: кварта — «через четыре», как [интервал], который проходит через четыре [звука] и ими ограничивается; квинта — «через пять»; октава — «через восемь» звуков или струн.» / EN “but these are neither without a middle nor incomposite, since they proceed through all the intervals mentioned above as intermediate ones; they are called intervals with respect to the ratio of the voice of the first note to the last, but they received their names from the number and order of the notes that lie in the middle: the fourth, “through four”, as that which proceeds through four [notes] and is bounded by them; the fifth, “through five”; the octave, “through eight” notes or strings.”
- **B** · The sounding from the first to the last, without an explicit relational comparison.
  - B · RU «но они не лишены промежуточных ступеней и не являются несоставными, поскольку проходят через все упомянутые промежуточные интервалы. Интервалами их называют по звучанию от первого звука до последнего, а эти названия они получили по числу и порядку звуков, находящихся между ними. Ведь кварта проходит через четыре, квинта — через пять, октава — через восемь звуков или струн и ими ограничена.» / EN “but these are neither without intervening steps nor incomposite, since they pass through all the intermediate intervals mentioned above. They are called intervals with reference to the sounding from the first note to the last. They receive these particular names, though, from the number and order of the notes in between, since the fourth extends through and is bounded by four notes or strings, the fifth by five, and the octave by eight.”
- **D** · The sound of the first note in relation to the last.
  - D · RU «Однако у них есть промежуточные [ступени], и к несоставным они не принадлежат, поскольку проходят через все названные выше промежуточные интервалы. Интервалами их называют по звучанию первого звука в отношении к последнему, а названия свои они получили по числу и порядку лежащих между ними звуков. Так, кварта проходит через четыре звука или струны и на том заканчивается, квинта — через пять, октава — через восемь.» / EN “These do not, however, lack an intermediate [step], nor are they incomposite, since they proceed through all the intervening intervals mentioned above. They are called intervals with reference to the sound of the first note in relation to the last. But their names come from the number and order of the notes between them: the fourth runs through four notes or strings and ends there, the fifth through five, and the octave through eight.”

**Verdict** · D preserves the comparative ad and leaves the degree of mathematical specification open. V19 establishes that ratio plus ad can be numerical here, while V43’s later explanation distinguishes this relation from the ordinal count.

- **Russian** · по отношению звучания первого звука к последнему
- **English** · with reference to the sound of the first note in relation to the last
- **Evidence** · Vinet 46.13–47.7, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · V12, V19, V43–V44 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. ratio [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=ratio>
- **Confidence and review** · medium · confirmed · for the editor

**Errors**

- **A · both · minor language** · “завершающаяся через четыре / terminates through four”. The construction makes the endpoint verb govern a traversal awkwardly and leaves the endpoints unclear. **Fix** · Say “spans four notes or strings and is bounded by them,” with the elliptical continuation for the fifth and octave. *(confirmed)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · open** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · pasôn nnncupata: sed hæc non sine me- / dio, neque non-composita sunt, quando / per media omnia supradicta interualla / incedunt: quæ ratione quidem vocis pri- / mi soni ad vltimum, interualla appellan- / tur: sed à numero tamen ordinéque, qui in / medio sunt, sonorum, hæc nomina rece- / perunt: Dià-tessáron, vt quod per quatuor: / Dià-pénte, per quinque: Dià-pasôn per / octo sonos aut chordas ingrediatur termi- / neturq́ue. Dià-pasôn autem per octo di-
  - **Proposal** · …they are called intervals in respect of the voice from the first note to the last… / …интервалами они называются в рассуждении голоса от первого звука до последнего…
  - A broader relation of the endpoints is preferable, but a quantitative reading of ratio remains possible in Vinet’s usage. Sounding from one note to the other loses the explicit comparison if read as a progression.

  - A broader relation of the endpoints is preferable, but a quantitative reading of ratio remains possible in Vinet’s usage. Sounding from one note to the other loses the explicit comparison if read as a progression.

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Retain the clarification that the counts of four, five and eight include both endpoints.

### V35 · Vinet 47.7–14

*Dia-pason autem per octo dicitur, tanquam in Octachordo intervallis omnibus comprehensis completisque, et hinc rursus repetitis, quemadmodum ex ipsa liquet appellationum ratione. Nam Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason posteriora intervalla vocantur.*

The phrase “through eight” is explained by the completion of the intervals within the octachord and their recurrence thereafter. The ensuing names show this recurrence by adjoining a fourth, a fifth or another octave to the octave.

- **Russian base** · A = D. Makes the role of the octachord clear and conveys the three subsequent names accurately.
- **English base** · B = D. Reads the naming explanation and the list idiomatically.

**Divergence · tanquam in Octachordo intervallis omnibus comprehensis completisque**

Does tanquam introduce the explanation or qualify the claim that the intervals are complete?

- **A, B, D** · The completion supplies the rationale for the name.
  - A · RU «так как в октахорде охвачены и исчерпаны все интервалы» / EN “on the ground that all the intervals are comprised and completed in the octachord”
  - B · RU «поскольку в октахорде заключены все интервалы» / EN “since all intervals are comprised and completed in the octachord”
  - D · RU «поскольку в октахорде заключены и исчерпаны все интервалы» / EN “on the understanding that all intervals are contained and completed in the octachord”
- **C** · As it were qualifies the statement of completeness.
  - C · RU «в октахорде как бы охватываются и завершаются все интервалы» / EN “in the octachord all the intervals are, as it were, comprised and completed”

**Verdict** · The apparent completion of the inventory at an octave is the explanatory ground for the name. The resumption of the intervals above it and the compound names supply the evidence Vinet adduces. 'On the understanding that' keeps the explanatory force while allowing for a represented premise.

- **Russian** · поскольку в октахорде заключены и исчерпаны все интервалы
- **English** · on the understanding that all intervals are contained and completed in the octachord
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 47.7–14 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. tamquam I–II [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=tamquam>
- **Confidence and review** · medium · added

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Mention Vinet’s bis Dia-pason beside Xylander’s single-word disdiapason.

### V36 · Vinet 47.15–20

*Consonat autem intervallum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pente secundum paraphonum: Dia-pason vero, et Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason secundum antiphonum.*

Fourth and fifth are consonant according to the paraphonic kind. The octave, octave plus fourth, octave plus fifth and double octave are consonant according to the antiphonic kind.

- **Russian base** · B. States the classification directly with the project’s consonance vocabulary.
- **English base** · C. The substantivized adjectives or an explicit “kind” signal classes of intervals.

**Divergence · secundum paraphonum … secundum antiphonum**

Names for kinds of consonance or names of procedures?

- **A, B, D** · Abstract nouns paraphony and antiphony. D’s Russian also supplies a class marker.
  - A · RU «Интервал кварты и [интервал] квинты консонируют по парафонии; октава же, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по антифонии.» / EN “The interval of the fourth, and that of the fifth, are consonant by paraphony; but the octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave by antiphony.”
  - B · RU «Кварта и квинта консонируют по парафонии, а октава, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по антифонии.» / EN “The fourth and fifth are consonant by paraphony. The octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave are consonant by antiphony.”
  - D · RU «Интервалы кварты и квинты консонируют по виду парафонии, а октава, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по виду антифонии.» / EN “The intervals of a fourth and a fifth are consonant according to paraphony. The octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave are consonant according to antiphony.”
- **C** · Substantivized adjectives with an explicit kind on first mention.
  - C · RU «Интервал кварты и [интервал] квинты консонируют по парафонному [типу], а октава, октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава — по антифонному.» / EN “The interval of the fourth and [that of] the fifth are consonant according to the paraphonic [kind]; the octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth and the double octave, according to the antiphonic.”

**Verdict** · C makes the class function of the neuter forms and the list most immediate. The abstract nouns remain viable if the terms are defined at first occurrence. Keep Vinet’s technical labels distinct from Xylander’s explanations.

- **Russian** · по парафонному [типу] … по антифонному
- **English** · according to the paraphonic [kind] … according to the antiphonic
- **Evidence** · Vinet 47.15–20, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · V37 and X31 [checked] · Theon of Smyrna, ed. Hiller, p. 48, antiphonic and paraphonic [from memory]
- **Confidence and review** · medium · confirmed · for the editor

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · Vinet gives two classes and illustrates the antiphonic one exclusively with octave pairs in V37.
- **A** · A note can situate the octave plus fourth against the differing Pythagorean and Ptolemaic positions recorded by Boethius.

**Editor questions**

- Agree a stable rendering of paraphonum and antiphonum across V36–V37 and X31–X32. A class adjective can avoid modern associations of antiphony with alternation.

### V37 · Vinet 47.21–48.7

*Differunt autem inter se Paraphonum et antiphonum, quod Paraphonum impari temporis spatio consonat, leniter quodammodo et apposite excipientibus inter se sonis, secundum proportiones et rationes aequabiliter: antiphonum vero aequo convenit temporis spacio, acuto cum gravi pariter consonante, ut octavo cum primo, undecimo cum quarto, duodecimo cum quinto, quintodecimo cum octavo, gravibus nimirum una cum acutis, aut acutis cum gravibus peraequa proportione simul ascendentibus vel descendentibus, in intensionibus remissionibusve.*

Paraphony is consonant in unequal time, with the notes smoothly and aptly taking one another up in regular proportion. Antiphony accords in equal time, with a high and low note sounding together. All four examples pair notes seven steps apart, and the paired high and low notes rise or fall together in equal proportion.

- **Russian base** · D. Brings out simultaneity and the ordered succession without altering the numbered pairs.
- **English base** · B. Retains the two time expressions and makes the simultaneity of antiphony explicit.

**Divergence · impari temporis spatio … aequo … temporis spacio**

Is the second phrase equal in span or the same coincident span?

- **A, B, C** · Render the equal/unequal terminology directly and express sounding together separately through pariter.
  - A · RU «Различаются же между собой парафония и антифония тем, что парафония консонирует в неравный промежуток времени, когда звуки неким образом мягко и уместно сменяют друг друга, равномерно, согласно пропорциям и отношениям; антифония же согласуется в равный промежуток времени, когда высокий [звук] консонирует вместе с низким, как восьмой с первым, одиннадцатый с четвёртым, двенадцатый с пятым, пятнадцатый с восьмым, — то есть когда низкие вместе с высокими или высокие с низкими в совершенно равной пропорции разом восходят или нисходят при повышениях или понижениях.» / EN “Paraphony and antiphony differ from each other in this, that paraphony is consonant in an unequal span of time, the notes taking one another up in a certain gentle and apt way, evenly, according to proportions and ratios; whereas antiphony accords in an equal span of time, the high being consonant together with the low, as the eighth with the first, the eleventh with the fourth, the twelfth with the fifth, the fifteenth with the eighth, the low, that is, together with the high, or the high with the low, ascending or descending at once in exactly equal proportion, in raisings or in lowerings.”
  - B · RU «Парафония и антифония различаются тем, что при парафонии звуки консонируют в неодинаковые промежутки времени, как бы плавно и стройно, равномерно сменяя друг друга сообразно пропорциям и отношениям. Антифония же даёт согласованность в равный промежуток времени, когда высокий звук консонирует с низким одновременно, например восьмой с первым, одиннадцатый с четвёртым, двенадцатый с пятым, пятнадцатый с восьмым. То есть низкие звуки вместе с высокими, или высокие с низкими, одновременно восходят или нисходят в совершенно равной пропорции при повышениях и понижениях.» / EN “Paraphony and antiphony differ in that paraphony is consonant over unequal spans of time, with the notes succeeding one another somewhat smoothly and fittingly, evenly according to proportions and ratios. Antiphony accords over an equal span of time, with a high note consonant simultaneously with a low one, as the eighth with the first, the eleventh with the fourth, the twelfth with the fifth, and the fifteenth with the eighth. That is, the low notes together with the high, or the high with the low, rise or descend simultaneously in precisely equal proportion as the pitch is raised or lowered.”
  - C · RU «Различаются же между собой парафонное и антифонное тем, что парафонное консонирует в неравный промежуток времени, когда звуки как бы мягко и уместно подхватывают друг друга, равномерно, согласно пропорциям и отношениям; антифонное же согласуется в равный промежуток времени, когда высокий [звук] консонирует вместе с низким, как восьмой с первым, одиннадцатый с четвёртым, двенадцатый с пятым, пятнадцатый с восьмым, — а именно, когда низкие вместе с высокими или высокие с низкими одновременно поднимаются или опускаются в совершенно равной пропорции, при повышениях или понижениях.» / EN “The paraphonic and the antiphonic differ from each other in that the paraphonic is consonant in an unequal space of time, the notes taking each other up, as it were, gently and fittingly, evenly, according to proportions and ratios; whereas the antiphonic accords in an equal space of time, the high [note] being consonant together with the low, as the eighth with the first, the eleventh with the fourth, the twelfth with the fifth, the fifteenth with the eighth; that is, the low notes together with the high, or the high with the low, rising or falling simultaneously in a perfectly equal proportion, in raisings or lowerings.”
- **D** · Preserves inequality for the first span and interprets the second as the same span.
  - D · RU «Парафония отличается от антифонии тем, что при парафонии звуки консонируют в неодинаковые промежутки времени, сменяя друг друга как бы плавно и к месту, с равномерным соблюдением пропорций и отношений. При антифонии же звуки согласуются в один и тот же промежуток времени: высокий звук консонирует одновременно с низким, например восьмой с первым, одиннадцатый с четвёртым, двенадцатый с пятым, пятнадцатый с восьмым. Именно так при повышениях или понижениях низкие звуки вместе с высокими или высокие с низкими одновременно поднимаются или опускаются в совершенно одинаковой пропорции.» / EN “Paraphony and antiphony differ in that in paraphony the notes are consonant at unequal intervals of time, taking one another up somewhat gently and fittingly, in even accordance with their proportions and ratios. In antiphony, on the other hand, they agree in the same interval of time, the high note being consonant with the low at the same moment, as the eighth with the first, the eleventh with the fourth, the twelfth with the fifth, and the fifteenth with the eighth. In raisings or lowerings [of pitch] the low notes rise or fall together with the high, or the high with the low, at the same time and in exactly equal proportion.”

**Verdict** · The wording gives unequal/equal spans, and excipientibus, pariter and simul make succession versus coincidence a strong contextual inference. D is reasonable as a contextual rendering, while ABC more closely preserve the parallel phrasing.

- **Russian** · в неодинаковые промежутки времени … в равный промежуток времени
- **English** · over unequal spans of time … over an equal span of time
- **Evidence** · Vinet 47.21–48.7, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. excipio II.B, succession [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:lat.ls.perseus-eng2-n16677/>
- **Confidence and review** · medium · confirmed · for the editor

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B · doubt · sound** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · Differunt autem inter se Paraphonum / et antiphonum, quòd Paraphonum impari / temporis spatio consonat, leniter quodam- / modo et appositè excipientibus inter se / sonis, secundum proportiones et rationes / æquabiliter: antiphonum verò æquo con- / uenit temporis spacio, acuto cùm graui / pariter consonante, vt octauo cum primo, / vndecimo cum quarto, duodecimo cum / quinto, quintodecimo cum octauo, graui- / bus nimirum vnà cum acutis, aut acutis cum / grauibus peræqua proportione simul ascen / dentibus vel descendentibus, in inten- / sionibus remissionibúsue.
  - **Proposal** · In paraphony the notes are consonant at separate times, and in antiphony at the same time. / При парафонии звуки консонируют друг за другом, при антифонии — в одно и то же время.
  - A defensible contextual interpretation of the temporal opposition, but the more literal wording retains spatio and leaves the time mechanism for the note.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet 47.21–48.7, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. excipio II.B, succession [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:lat.ls.perseus-eng2-n16677/>
- **D · doubt · sound** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · Differunt autem inter se Paraphonum / et antiphonum, quòd Paraphonum impari / temporis spatio consonat, leniter quodam- / modo et appositè excipientibus inter se / sonis, secundum proportiones et rationes / æquabiliter: antiphonum verò æquo con- / uenit temporis spacio, acuto cùm graui / pariter consonante, vt octauo cum primo, / vndecimo cum quarto, duodecimo cum / quinto, quintodecimo cum octauo, graui- / bus nimirum vnà cum acutis, aut acutis cum / grauibus peræqua proportione simul ascen / dentibus vel descendentibus, in inten- / sionibus remissionibúsue.
  - **Proposal** · in paraphony the notes are consonant at different times … but in antiphony simultaneously / при парафонии звуки консонируют в разное время … при антифонии же — одновременно
  - A defensible contextual interpretation of the temporal opposition, but the more literal wording retains spatio and leaves the time mechanism for the note.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet 47.21–48.7, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. excipio II.B, succession [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:lat.ls.perseus-eng2-n16677/>
- **C · doubt · unsound** (modified)
  - **Printed** · Differunt autem inter se Paraphonum / et antiphonum, quòd Paraphonum impari / temporis spatio consonat, leniter quodam- / modo et appositè excipientibus inter se / sonis, secundum proportiones et rationes / æquabiliter: antiphonum verò æquo con- / uenit temporis spacio, acuto cùm graui / pariter consonante, vt octauo cum primo, / vndecimo cum quarto, duodecimo cum / quinto, quintodecimo cum octauo, graui- / bus nimirum vnà cum acutis, aut acutis cum / grauibus peræqua proportione simul ascen / dentibus vel descendentibus, in inten- / sionibus remissionibúsue.
  - **Proposal** · the high note being equally consonant with the low / когда высокий звук консонирует с низким одинаково
  - Equally is a lexical possibility, but the high note with the low over equal time and the subsequent simul favor simultaneously in this clause.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 47.21–48.7 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. excipio II.B.2.b [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=excipio> · Lewis & Short, s.v. par, adv. pariter, temporal meaning [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=pariter>
- **A · doubt · unsound** (added)
  - **Printed** · Differunt autem inter se Paraphonum / et antiphonum, quòd Paraphonum impari / temporis spatio consonat, leniter quodam- / modo et appositè excipientibus inter se / sonis, secundum proportiones et rationes / æquabiliter: antiphonum verò æquo con- / uenit temporis spacio, acuto cùm graui / pariter consonante, vt octauo cum primo, / vndecimo cum quarto, duodecimo cum / quinto, quintodecimo cum octauo, graui- / bus nimirum vnà cum acutis, aut acutis cum / grauibus peræqua proportione simul ascen / dentibus vel descendentibus, in inten- / sionibus remissionibúsue.
  - **Proposal** · according to proportions and ratios [that are] equal
  - The adverb aequabiliter characterizes the action of the notes, whether their succession or the regular observance of ratios. The Latin does not predicate equality of those ratios as such. Vinet uses peraequa proportion(e) expressly on the antiphonic side of the contrast.

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Keep the computed octave span of each pair and explain the possible duration/simultaneity distinction.
- **C** · Keep the contextual sense of excipere as notes taking one another up in succession.

**Editor questions**

- Use a note to distinguish a close rendering of unequal/equal spans from its likely musical implication of succession/coincidence.

### V38 · Vinet 48.8–11

*Porro tonus et hemitonium et diesis per se ipsa considerata, principium quidem consonantiae sunt, sed nondum tamen consonantia.*

The tone, semitone and diesis taken in themselves form the beginning of consonance but are not yet consonance.

- **Russian base** · D. Idiomatic and keeps the singular predicate and the distinction between beginning and realization.
- **English base** · D. “Constitute the beginning” conveys the collective predicate and the following qualification.

### V39 · Vinet 48.12–20

*Conficitur autem Dia-tessaron duobus tonis et hemitonio et modica praeterea toni parte, quae diesi minor, nomine vacat: Dia-pente vero tribus tonis et hemitonio: et Dia-pason sex tonis: ac Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, tonis octo et hemitonio: Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, novem tonis et hemitonio: bis Dia-pason tonis duodecim.*

The fourth consists of two tones, a semitone and a further unnamed portion of a tone smaller than a diesis. The fifth takes three tones and a semitone, the octave six tones, the octave plus fourth eight tones and a semitone, the octave plus fifth nine tones and a semitone, and the double octave twelve tones.

- **Russian base** · B = C. Preserves the whole list and the extra portion in the fourth without adding a causal relation.
- **English base** · B = D. Clear, complete account of the interval components.

**Errors**

- **D · ru · minor addition** · “меньше диесы и потому не имеет названия”. The small part is explicitly below a diesis and unnamed. Causation is a possible inference from the predicative adjective with an unexpressed est, but D turns that inference into an explicit assertion. The Latin gives no causal particle. **Fix** · малая часть тона, которая меньше диесы и не имеет названия *(modified)*

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Retain the discrepancy in the additive counts and the separate check against numerical ratios.
- **D** · Point to V55–V56, where the extra portion is absent from the account of the diatonic fourth.

### V40 · Vinet 48.21–25

*Ratio autem intervalli Dia-tessaron, est sesquitertia: Dia-pente, sesquialtera: Dia-pason, duplex: Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron duplex-superpartiens: Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, triplex:*

The named ratios of the fourth, fifth, octave, octave plus fourth and octave plus fifth are respectively 4:3, 3:2, 2:1, 8:3 and 3:1. The list continues into V41.

- **Russian base** · C. Complete, correctly bracketed ratio names and a readable list.
- **English base** · B. Clear elliptical series with each prescribed ratio name and bracket.

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · Distinguish Vinet’s duplex-superpartiens from Xylander’s specification in thirds and explain the Boethian name for 8:3.

### V41 · Vinet 48.25–26

*Bis Dia-pason, quadruplex:*

The double octave is quadruple [4:1]. The numerical illustration of the list follows in V42.

- **Russian base** · A = C. Both preserve the elliptical list and the transition into the example.
- **English base** · A = C. Both preserve the original list structure and its continuation.

### V42 · Vinet 48.26–49.6

*ut exempli gratia in numeris, primus proponatur nobis terminus, numerus sex, cuius sesquitertius est plane octo: sesquialter, novem: duplex, duodecim: duplex-superpartiens, sedecim, sex bis continens, et duas eius partes: triplex, octodecim: quadruplex, viginti quatuor.*

With six as first term, the six larger terms are eight, nine, twelve, sixteen, eighteen and twenty-four, giving 4:3, 3:2, 2:1, 8:3, 3:1 and 4:1. Sixteen contains twice six and two of its parts, i.e. two thirds of six.

- **Russian base** · C. Spells out the fraction encoded in the Latin and checks all ratios.
- **English base** · C. Explicit about the conventional fraction and keeps the link with the preceding list.

**Divergence · duas eius partes**

Should the conventional fractional expression be explicit in the translation?

- **A, B, D** · Two parts of six, with the fraction unpacked in notes or checks.
  - A · RU «так, например, в числах: пусть первым членом у нас будет число шесть; сверхтретное [4:3] к нему — это, очевидно, восемь; полуторное [3:2] — девять; двукратное [2:1] — двенадцать; двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] — шестнадцать, содержащее шесть дважды и ещё две его части; трёхкратное [3:1] — восемнадцать; четырёхкратное [4:1] — двадцать четыре.» / EN “as, for example, in numbers: let the first term set before us be the number six; its sesquitertian [4:3] is plainly eight; its sesquialter [3:2], nine; its duple [2:1], twelve; its duple superpartient [8:3], sixteen, which contains six twice and two parts of it; its triple [3:1], eighteen; its quadruple [4:1], twenty-four.”
  - B · RU «Для числового примера возьмём первым членом число шесть. Сверхтретное [4:3] к нему — ровно восемь, полуторное [3:2] — девять, двукратное [2:1] — двенадцать, двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] — шестнадцать, ибо оно содержит шесть дважды и ещё две его доли, трёхкратное [3:1] — восемнадцать, четырёхкратное [4:1] — двадцать четыре.» / EN “To give a numerical example, let the number six be set down as our first term. Exactly eight stands to it in a sesquitertian [4:3] ratio, nine in a sesquialter [3:2], twelve in a duple [2:1], sixteen in a duple superpartient [8:3], for it contains six twice and two of its parts besides, eighteen in a triple [3:1], and twenty-four in a quadruple [4:1].”
  - D · RU «Пусть, например, первым членом числового ряда будет шесть. Тогда по отношению к нему восемь даёт сверхтретное [4:3], девять — полуторное [3:2], двенадцать — двукратное [2:1], шестнадцать — двукратное сверхчастное [8:3], поскольку заключает в себе шесть дважды и ещё две его части, восемнадцать — трёхкратное [3:1], двадцать четыре — четырёхкратное [4:1].» / EN “As a numerical example let us take six as our first term. Its sesquitertian [4:3] is plainly eight, its sesquialter [3:2] nine, its duple [2:1] twelve, its duple superpartient [8:3] sixteen, containing six twice and two parts of it besides, its triple [3:1] eighteen, and its quadruple [4:1] twenty-four.”
- **C** · Two thirds of six in the running text.
  - C · RU «как, например, в числах: пусть первым членом нам будет предложено число шесть; сверхтретное [4:3] к нему — разумеется, восемь, полуторное [3:2] — девять, двукратное [2:1] — двенадцать, двукратное сверхчастное [8:3] — шестнадцать, содержащее шесть дважды и [ещё] две трети его, трёхкратное [3:1] — восемнадцать, четырёхкратное [4:1] — двадцать четыре.» / EN “as, for instance, in numbers: let the first term proposed to us be the number six; its sesquitertian [4:3] is plainly eight; its sesquialter [3:2], nine; its duple [2:1], twelve; its duple superpartient [8:3], sixteen, containing six twice and two thirds of it [besides]; its triple [3:1], eighteen; its quadruple [4:1], twenty-four.”

**Verdict** · C gives the fraction intended by the idiom and forced by the example, since 16 − 12 = 4, which is two thirds of six. The literal rendering remains serviceable with an immediate explanatory note.

- **Russian** · и ещё две трети от шести
- **English** · and two thirds of it besides
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan p. 49 [checked] · Allen & Greenough, New Latin Grammar §135e n.2, duae partes [checked] <https://dcc.dickinson.edu/grammar/latin/uses-cardinals-and-ordinals> · Boethius, De institutione arithmetica I.28 and I.31, ed. Friedlein (1867), application to 5:3 and 16:6 [checked] <https://la.wikisource.org/wiki/De_Arithmetica/Liber_primus>
- **Confidence and review** · high · modified

**Errors**

- **D · notes · major note-claim** · “The numerals read 4 and 3, 3 and 2, 2 and 1, though some sorts appear turned or indistinct.”. The first 3 is an inferred count of three subdivisions, not a printed numeral. The supplied scan of p. 49 shows five numerals: 4, 3, 2, 2 and 1. **Fix** · List the five visible numerals separately from the three ratios 4:3, 3:2 and 2:1, which follow from the lengths and marked divisions. *(added)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C · doubt · sound** (modified)
  - **Printed** · pason, quadruplex: vt exempli gratia in nu- / meris, primus proponatur nobis terminus, / numerus sex, cuius sesquitertius est pla- / nè octo: sesquialter, nouem: duplex, duo- / decim: duplex-superpartiens, sedecim, / sex bis continens, et duas eius partes: tri- / plex, octodecim: quadruplex, viginti / quatuor.
  - **Proposal** · containing six twice and two parts of it / содержащее шесть дважды и две его части
  - “Two parts” reproduces the words, and the ratio and the Latin fractional idiom fix the unit as thirds. An explicit fraction is better in the body, and the alternative works with a gloss.

Friedlein (1867), application to 5:3 and 16:6 [checked] <https://la.wikisource.org/wiki/De_Arithmetica/Liber_primus>

  - **Proposal** · Record the diagram in the description of the text; it is not represented in the segments.

  - **Proposal** · Record the figure (it illustrates the ratios 4:3, 3:2, 2:1 after V42).

The lower first line appears to start with the same turned sort that stands on the upper line at the midpoint; G in the proposed GF is a restoration, not a secure reading of the printed sort.

### V43 · Vinet 49.7–10

*Verumtamen sciendum, rationes intervallorum, non ex sonorum, aut ex chordarum numero iudicari; sed ex intentione vocis a gravi ad acutius:*

Interval ratios are assessed from the increase in sounding pitch from low to higher. The note or string count gives a different measure.

- **Russian base** · D. Conveys the rise from low to higher pitch without implying vocal strain.
- **English base** · D. Keeps the full two-part argument while making the direction in pitch intelligible.

**Divergence · ex intentione vocis a gravi ad acutius**

Is the determining property vocal tension or the rise in pitch?

- **A, C** · Tension of the voice from low to higher.
  - A · RU «Следует, однако, знать, что об отношениях интервалов судят не по числу звуков или струн, а по напряжению голоса от низкого к более высокому:» / EN “It must be known, however, that the ratios of the intervals are judged not from the number of the notes or of the strings, but from the tension of the voice from low to higher:”
  - C · RU «Однако следует знать, что об отношениях интервалов судят не по числу звуков или струн, а по напряжению голоса от низкого к более высокому;» / EN “However, it must be understood that the ratios of intervals are judged not from the number of the notes or of the strings, but from the tension of the voice from low to higher;”
- **B, D** · Rise in sounding pitch from low to higher.
  - B · RU «Однако следует знать, что отношения интервалов определяют не по числу звуков или струн, а по повышению звучания от низкого к более высокому,» / EN “One should nevertheless know that the ratios of intervals are assessed by the rise in pitch of the sound from low to higher, independently of the number of notes or strings,”
  - D · RU «Нужно, однако, знать, что об отношениях интервалов судят не по числу звуков или струн, а по повышению голоса от низкого звучания к более высокому:» / EN “One must understand, however, that the ratios of intervals are judged not by the number of notes or strings but by the rise of the voice from a low pitch to a higher one,”

**Verdict** · BD communicate the operative acoustic direction given by a gravi ad acutius and V45. Literal tension is lexically possible and belongs in the note. The text concerns the relation exposed when the pitch is raised.

- **Russian** · по повышению звучания от низкого к более высокому
- **English** · by the rise in pitch from low to higher
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 49, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intentio I and I.B [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=intentio> · V45 [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · confirmed

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · text issue · open** (modified · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · intentio-|ne (49.9–10), against intensione (49.19; 50.6; 50.11; 50.22) and intensio (42.16)
  - **Proposal** · Keep intentione as printed; treat it as a spelling variant of intensione.
  - Keeping intentione is sound. The two forms are securely printed and have overlapping senses here, but the distinct derivational forms should not be reduced to a spelling variant by editorial declaration. Note the difference and translate each by context.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 49, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intentio I and I.B [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=intentio>
- **B · text issue · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · ex intentio- / ne uocis
  - **Proposal** · Retain intentione.
  - The printed intentione is defensible Latin in this context and need not be conformed to intensione only a few lines later. A note may distinguish related formations without asserting that they are always interchangeable.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 49, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intentio I and I.B [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=intentio>

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Record intentione in V43 and intensione in V45 and explain their overlap in this pitch context.

### V44 · Vinet 49.10–18

*quandoquidem intervallum Dia-pente, cuius est ratio sesquialtera, sesquialterum minime videtur ex ratione numeri sonorum, quo numero superat sonos, qui ipsum praecedunt. Nam primi soni quincuplex invenitur: secundi vero, duplum sesquialterum: tertii, superpartiens: quarti, sesquiquartum: nullius vero sesquialterum demonstrari potest.*

The fifth has the pitch ratio 3:2, although its ordinal position five gives 5:1, 5:2, 5:3 and 5:4 when compared with each of the first four positions. None is 3:2.

- **Russian base** · B. Clarifies the ordinal comparison and consistently brackets the ratios.
- **English base** · D. The bracketed subject clarifies the ordinal example and the final sentence draws out the demonstration.

**Divergence · ex ratione numeri sonorum**

Does ratio retain the same force in the expression for the calculation by ordinal number?

- **A, B, D** · Reckoning by the number or count of notes.
  - A · RU «ведь интервал квинты, отношение которого — полуторное [3:2], отнюдь не представляется полуторным [3:2], если считать по числу звуков — тому числу, которым он превосходит предшествующие ему звуки. Ибо по отношению к первому звуку он оказывается пятикратным [5:1], ко второму — двукратным полуторным [5:2], к третьему — сверхчастным [5:3], к четвёртому — сверхчетвертным [5:4]; полуторным же [3:2] его нельзя показать ни к одному.» / EN “since the interval of the fifth, whose ratio is sesquialter [3:2], by no means appears sesquialter [3:2] on a reckoning by the number of the notes, the number by which it surpasses the notes that precede it. For it is found to be the quintuple [5:1] of the first note; of the second, the duple sesquialter [5:2]; of the third, the superpartient [5:3]; of the fourth, the sesquiquartan [5:4]; but it cannot be shown to be the sesquialter [3:2] of any.”
  - B · RU «поскольку интервал квинты, у которого отношение полуторное [3:2], никак не оказывается полуторным [3:2] по счёту звуков, если сравнивать его номер с номерами предшествующих звуков. К первому звуку отношение оказывается пятикратным [5:1], ко второму — двукратно-полуторным [5:2], к третьему — сверхчастным [5:3], к четвёртому — сверхчетвертным [5:4]. Ни к одному из них полуторного [3:2] отношения показать нельзя.» / EN “since the fifth, whose ratio is sesquialter [3:2], does not appear sesquialter [3:2] by the count of notes, that is, the number by which it exceeds the preceding notes. Its ratio to the first note is found to be quintuple [5:1], to the second duple sesquialter [5:2], to the third superpartient [5:3], and to the fourth sesquiquartan [5:4]. It cannot be shown to be sesquialter [3:2] with respect to any of them.”
  - D · RU «ведь интервал квинты, отношение которого полуторное [3:2], вовсе не оказывается полуторным [3:2], если исходить из числа звуков и смотреть, насколько [пятый звук] по числу превосходит предшествующие. Его отношение к первому звуку оказывается пятикратным [5:1], ко второму — двукратным полуторным [5:2], к третьему — сверхчастным [5:3], к четвёртому — сверхчетвертным [5:4], и ни к одному из них оно, как можно показать, не будет полуторным [3:2].» / EN “since the interval of a fifth, whose ratio is sesquialter [3:2], does not appear sesquialter [3:2] at all if judged by the number of the notes, the number by which [the fifth note] exceeds those preceding it. It proves to be quintuple [5:1] in relation to the first note, duple sesquialter [5:2] to the second, superpartient [5:3] to the third, and sesquiquartan [5:4] to the fourth. It cannot be shown to be sesquialter [3:2] in relation to any of them.”
- **C** · Ratio of the number of notes.
  - C · RU «ведь интервал квинты, отношение которого полуторное [3:2], вовсе не оказывается полуторным [3:2] по отношению числа звуков — того числа, которым он превосходит предшествующие ему звуки. Ибо относительно первого звука он оказывается пятикратным [5:1], относительно второго — двукратным полуторным [5:2], третьего — сверхчастным [5:3], четвёртого — сверхчетвертным [5:4]; полуторным же [3:2] его нельзя показать ни относительно одного.» / EN “since the interval of the fifth, whose ratio is sesquialter [3:2], by no means appears sesquialter [3:2] from the ratio of the number of notes, the number by which it exceeds the notes that precede it. For with respect to the first note it is found to be quintuple [5:1]; to the second, duple sesquialter [5:2]; to the third, superpartient [5:3]; to the fourth, sesquiquartan [5:4]; but with respect to none can it be shown to be sesquialter [3:2].”

**Verdict** · ABD give the idiomatic force of ex ratione here. In the demonstration a ratio is indeed calculated each time, but the number used for the reckoning is an ordinal position and the general expression does not specify a single ratio.

- **Russian** · если судить по числу звуков
- **English** · if judged by the count of the notes
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 49, supplied scan [checked] · V43, V45 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. ratio [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=ratio>
- **Confidence and review** · high · confirmed

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · unsound** (modified)
  - **Printed** · ne vocis à graui ad acutius: quandoquidem / interuallum Dià-pénte, cuius est ratio ses / quialtera, sesquialterum minimè videtur / ex ratione numeri sonorum, quo numero / superat sonos, qui ipsum præcedunt. Nam / primi soni quincuplex inuenitur: secundi / verò, duplum sesquialterum: tertij, super- / partiens: quarti, sesquiquartum: nullius / verò sesquialterum demonstrari potest.
  - **Proposal** · …by no means appears sesquialter [3:2] by the ratio of the number of the notes… / …отнюдь не представляется полуторным [3:2] по отношению числа звуков…
  - A numerical reading can make sense as the method of comparing positions, but the alternative's singular definite 'ratio of the number' obscures the operation. Each of four different ratios uses the fifth note's position as the numerator. Translate ex ratione as reckoning or calculation, and explain the comparisons.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 49.10–18 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. ratio, opening senses [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=ratio> · Boethius, De institutione arithmetica I.28 and I.31, ed. Friedlein (1867), application to 5:3 and 16:6 [checked] <https://la.wikisource.org/wiki/De_Arithmetica/Liber_primus>

**Notes to keep**

- **A** · Explain both exceptional ratio names using the comparisons of the ordinal five with two and three.
- **C** · Keep Boethius’s more specific denominations as comparisons to Vinet’s own naming.

### V45 · Vinet 49.19–20

*Ab intensione itaque acuti, non a sonorum numero, sesquialterum censetur.*

The fifth is counted sesquialter [3:2] because of the rise to the upper pitch, as opposed to the number of notes.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Both identify the acoustic rise clearly and give the required ratio.
- **English base** · D. A clean statement of the conclusion that also preserves the direct contrast with the ordinal count.

**Divergence · ab intensione … acuti**

Does the phrase identify the tension of a particular high note or the increase toward higher pitch?

- **A** · Tension of the high, with acuti substantivized.
  - A · RU «Итак, полуторным [3:2] он считается по напряжению высокого, а не по числу звуков.» / EN “It is therefore from the tension of the high, not from the number of the notes, that it is reckoned sesquialter [3:2].”
- **B, D** · Rise in sounding pitch.
  - B · RU «Следовательно, полуторным [3:2] его считают по повышению звука, а не по числу звуков.» / EN “It is therefore reckoned sesquialter [3:2] by the rise in pitch, without reference to the number of notes.”
  - D · RU «Итак, полуторным [3:2] его считают по повышению звука, а не по числу звуков.» / EN “It is reckoned sesquialter [3:2], then, by the rise in pitch, not by the number of notes.”
- **C** · Tension of the high [note], with sonus understood.
  - C · RU «Итак, полуторным [3:2] он признаётся по напряжению высокого [звука], а не по числу звуков.» / EN “So it is judged sesquialter [3:2] from the tension of the high [note], not from the number of notes.”

**Verdict** · The directional explanation in V43 favors the pitch rise of BD. The form acuti also admits an adjectival reading with sonus understood, but the comparison concerns the movement to the high pitch, rather than an isolated high note’s physical tension.

- **Russian** · по повышению звучания до высокого звука
- **English** · from the rise to the higher pitch
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 49, supplied scan [checked] · V43 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intentio, literal sense and increase [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:lat.ls.perseus-eng2-n24146/>
- **Confidence and review** · high · confirmed

**Errors**

- **A · both · minor language** · “по напряжению высокого / from the tension of the high”. The unsupported substantive is opaque as a reference to pitch, particularly in Russian. **Fix** · Specify the rise to the higher pitch. *(confirmed)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · open** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · Ab intensione itaque acuti, non à sonorum / numero, sesquialterum censetur. Idem
  - **Proposal** · It is therefore from the tension of the high [note], not from the number of the notes, that it is reckoned sesquialter [3:2]. / Итак, полуторным [3:2] он считается по напряжению высокого [звука], а не по числу звуков.
  - An implied sonus is grammatically possible. The abstract wording of V43 makes the comparative rise the point of the conclusion. Both readings can be expressed without committing to the formal antecedent.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 49, supplied scan [checked] · V43 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intentio, literal sense and increase [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:lat.ls.perseus-eng2-n24146/>

### V46 · Vinet 49.20–50.2

*Idem autem et in reliquis quoque rationibus nobis licebit considerare.*

The same procedure or observation can be considered in the remaining ratios.

- **Russian base** · B. Natural and leaves idem dependent on the argument just made.
- **English base** · D. States the extension without adding a numeric conclusion.

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Use the ordinal counterexamples to show that the general principle is stronger than the claim that the correct ratio can never occur accidentally among the ordinal ratios.

### V47 · Vinet 50.3–11

*Inventae autem hae sunt intervallorum rationes et deprehensae, ex dispari chordarum vel longitudine vel crassitudine: vel ea, quae per torsionem claviculorum fit, intensione; aut, quod compertum magis, ex ponderum e nervis appensione: In iis vero quae inflata sonant, aut ex fistularum amplitudine, aut ex intensione spiritus ac remissione.*

The interval ratios were discovered and established by unequal string lengths or thicknesses, tension produced by turning pegs, or, as more certainly established, by suspending weights from strings. In wind instruments the means were pipe dimensions and the strengthening or slackening of breath.

- **Russian base** · D. Distinguishes string tension from variation in the strength of the air stream.
- **English base** · B. Communicates all methods and keeps the pipe dimension open.

**Divergence · ex fistularum amplitudine**

Does amplitudo specify the width of the pipes or leave the relevant dimension open?

- **A, C** · Width of the pipes.
  - A · RU «Найдены же и обнаружены были эти отношения интервалов по неравной длине или толщине струн; или по тому напряжению, которое создаётся вращением колков; или — что установлено надёжнее — по подвешиванию грузов к струнам; а в [инструментах], звучащих от вдувания, — либо по ширине трубок, либо по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания.» / EN “These ratios of the intervals were found and detected from the unequal length or thickness of strings; or from that tension which is produced by the turning of the pegs; or, what is better ascertained, from the hanging of weights from the strings; and in those [instruments] that sound when blown, either from the width of the pipes or from the tension and slackening of the breath.”
  - C · RU «Найдены же и установлены эти отношения интервалов по неравной длине или толщине струн, или по тому напряжению, которое производится закручиванием колков; или же — что выяснено вернее — по подвешиванию грузов к струнам; а у [инструментов], которые звучат от вдувания, — либо по ширине трубок, либо по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания.» / EN “These ratios of intervals were found and established from the unequal length or thickness of strings; or from the tension produced by the twisting of the pegs; or, what is more reliably ascertained, from the hanging of weights from the strings; and in those [instruments] that sound by being blown, either from the width of the pipes or from the tension and slackening of the breath.”
- **B, D** · Dimensions or size of the pipes.
  - B · RU «Эти отношения интервалов были найдены и установлены по различию в длине или толщине струн, по натяжению, возникающему при повороте колков, или, что надёжнее установлено, по грузам, подвешенным к струнам. В инструментах, звучащих от вдувания воздуха, их установили либо по величине трубок, либо по усилению и ослаблению дыхания.» / EN “These ratios of intervals were discovered and determined from unequal lengths or thicknesses of strings, or from the tension imparted by turning the pegs, or, what is more securely established, from weights hung from strings. In instruments that sound when blown, they were established either from the dimensions of the pipes or by strengthening and weakening the breath.”
  - D · RU «Эти отношения интервалов были найдены и установлены по неодинаковой длине или толщине струн, по их натяжению при повороте колков или, что достовернее известно, при подвешивании к струнам грузов. У инструментов, звучащих, когда в них дуют, — либо по величине трубок, либо по усилению и ослаблению струи воздуха.» / EN “These interval ratios were discovered and ascertained from inequalities in the length or thickness of strings, or from the tension obtained by turning the tuning pegs, or, as is better established, by hanging weights from the strings. In instruments that sound when blown, they were found either from the size of the pipes or from the strengthening and slackening of the breath.”

**Verdict** · Amplitudo admits both width and general dimensions. Theon explicitly mentions the breadth of cavities, which supports a bore interpretation as a parallel. Vinet’s Latin names the pipe and leaves the axis implicit. Retain the broader term in the base or state the assumed dimension in commentary.

- **Russian** · по размерам трубок
- **English** · from the dimensions of the pipes
- **Evidence** · Vinet 50.3–11, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 50, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. amplitudo I [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=amplitudo> · Theon of Smyrna, Expositio, ed. Hiller pp. 56–57, specifically p. 57 lines 1–7 [checked] <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenGreekAndLatin/First1KGreek/master/data/tlg1724/tlg001/tlg1724.tlg001.1st1K-grc1.xml#L1944-L1951>

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · unsound** (modified)
  - **Printed** · Inuentæ autem hæ sunt interuallorum / rationes et deprehensæ, ex dispari chor- / datum vel longitudine vel crassitudine: / vel ea, quæ per torsionem clauiculorum / fit, intensione; aut, quod compertum ma- / gis, ex ponderum è neruis appensione: In / iis verò quæ inflata sonant, aut ex fistula- / rum amplitudine, aut ex intensione spiri- / tus ac remissione.
  - **Proposal** · (a) 'what is better ascertained'; (b) 'what has rather been found [to be the case]'.
  - Better ascertained renders the comparative degree of certain knowledge. The weaker 'rather been found to be the case' is possible as a loose paraphrase of comparative preference, but it suppresses what is being compared, namely how securely each procedure established the result. The question of frequency is unexpressed.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 50.3–11 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. comperio, opening senses [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=comperio>
- **C · doubt · open** (modified)
  - **Printed** · Inuentæ autem hæ sunt interuallorum / rationes et deprehensæ, ex dispari chor- / datum vel longitudine vel crassitudine: / vel ea, quæ per torsionem clauiculorum / fit, intensione; aut, quod compertum ma- / gis, ex ponderum è neruis appensione: In / iis verò quæ inflata sonant, aut ex fistula- / rum amplitudine, aut ex intensione spiri- / tus ac remissione.
  - **Proposal** · or, what is better known, from the hanging of weights from the strings / или же, что известнее, по подвешиванию грузов к струнам
  - Better known can mean better established, which is a sound possibility. Understood as more widely known, the alternative adds a claim about diffusion. Comperio emphasizes finding out with certainty. Retain established in the base.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 50.3–11 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. comperio, opening senses [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=comperio>
- **D · doubt · open** (modified · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · Inuentæ autem hæ sunt interuallorum / rationes et deprehensæ, ex dispari chor- / datum vel longitudine vel crassitudine: / vel ea, quæ per torsionem clauiculorum / fit, intensione; aut, quod compertum ma- / gis, ex ponderum è neruis appensione: In / iis verò quæ inflata sonant, aut ex fistula- / rum amplitudine, aut ex intensione spiri- / tus ac remissione.
  - **Proposal** · from the width of the pipes’ bores / по ширине просвета трубок
  - Theon’s specification of cavity breadth supports the proposed width of the bore. Vinet’s Latin permits that dimension without specifying it.

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Keep the identification of the pegs and the two Latin words for string.
- **D** · If the physical assumptions are explained, retain the check that at equal length and linear density a 3:2 pitch ratio requires 9:4 tension.

### V48 · Vinet 50.12–13

*Caput IV. De Systematis.*

Chapter IV, On Systems.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Both give the chapter and topic directly.
- **English base** · B = D. Both read naturally as headings.

### V49 · Vinet 50.14–51.3

*Systematum autem hoc quidem Tetrachordum est, quod omnes intervalli Dia-tessaron continet facultates: aliud vero Pentachordum, quod intervalli Dia-pente facultates complectitur: aliud Octachordum, quod totam intervalli Dia-pason vim continet: aliud deinceps Heckaedecachordum, bis Dia-pason universam comprehendens intensionem, duplicesque habens facultates: eas enim, quas modo diximus, atque intervallorum Dia-pason Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason facultates in se continet.*

The tetrachord contains the fourth’s capacities, the pentachord the fifth’s, and the octachord the entire force of the octave. The sixteen-string system spans the full double octave, with two sets of capacities: those just listed and those of the octave plus fourth, octave plus fifth, and double octave.

- **Russian base** · B. Accurate on the double octave’s pitch compass and preserves the twofold structure.
- **English base** · D. Makes the contrast between the whole octave’s power and the double octave’s twofold capacity readable.

**Divergence · universam comprehendens intensionem**

What does intensio measure in the description of the sixteen-string system?

- **A, C** · The complete tension of the double octave.
  - A · RU «Из систем же одна — тетрахорд, содержащий все возможности интервала кварты; другая — пентахорд, охватывающий возможности интервала квинты; ещё одна — октахорд, содержащий всю силу интервала октавы; затем ещё одна — шестнадцатиструнная [система], которая объемлет всё напряжение двойной октавы и обладает двоякими возможностями: ведь она содержит в себе и те, о которых мы только что сказали, и возможности интервалов октавы с квартой, октавы с квинтой и двойной октавы.» / EN “Of the systems, one is the tetrachord, which contains all the powers of the interval of the fourth; another the pentachord, which embraces the powers of the interval of the fifth; another the octachord, which contains the whole force of the interval of the octave; another, next, the sixteen-stringed [system], comprising the entire tension of the double octave and having twofold powers: for it contains in itself those which we have just mentioned and also the powers of the intervals of the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave.”
  - C · RU «Из систем же одна — тетрахорд, который содержит все возможности интервала кварты; другая — пентахорд, который охватывает возможности интервала квинты; ещё одна — октахорд, который содержит всю силу интервала октавы; далее ещё одна — шестнадцатиструнная [система], охватывающая всё напряжение двойной октавы и имеющая двойные возможности: ведь она содержит в себе и те, о которых мы только что сказали, и возможности интервалов октавы с квартой, октавы с квинтой и двойной октавы.» / EN “Of the systems, one is the tetrachord, which contains all the capacities of the interval of the fourth; another the pentachord, which embraces the capacities of the interval of the fifth; another the octachord, which contains the whole force of the interval of the octave; another, next, the sixteen-string [system], comprising the entire tension of the double octave and having double capacities: for it contains in itself those that we have just mentioned, and the capacities of the intervals of the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave.”
- **B** · The whole range in pitch.
  - B · RU «Одна из систем — тетрахорд, вмещающий все возможности интервала кварты. Другая — пентахорд, охватывающий возможности интервала квинты. Ещё одна — октахорд, заключающий в себе всю силу интервала октавы. Далее идёт шестнадцатиструнная система, охватывающая всю высотную протяжённость двойной октавы и обладающая двоякими возможностями: она вмещает как те, о которых мы только что сказали, так и возможности интервалов октавы с квартой, октавы с квинтой и двойной октавы.» / EN “One system is the tetrachord, which contains all the capacities of the fourth. Another is the pentachord, which embraces the capacities of the fifth. Another is the octachord, which contains the entire capacity of the octave. Next comes the sixteen-string system, which spans the full pitch range of the double octave and has two kinds of capacity. For it contains both those just mentioned and the capacities of the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave.”
- **D** · The full rise from one end of the system to the other.
  - D · RU «Одна из систем — тетрахорд, заключающий в себе все возможности кварты. Другая — пентахорд, охватывающий возможности квинты. Ещё одна — октахорд, вмещающий всю силу октавы. Далее следует шестнадцатиструнная система, охватывающая весь подъём на двойную октаву. Её возможности двояки. Она заключает в себе как только что перечисленные возможности, так и возможности октавы с квартой, октавы с квинтой и двойной октавы.» / EN “One of the systems is the tetrachord, which contains all the capacities of the fourth. Another is the pentachord, which embraces the capacities of the fifth. Another is the octachord, which contains the full power of the octave. Next comes the sixteen-string system, which spans the full rise of the double octave and has a twofold capacity. It contains both the capacities we have just described and those of the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave.”

**Verdict** · B states the system’s compass as a property, which is the point of comprehendens. D captures the same full extent in directional terms. A and C are lexically close but need the contextual gloss to avoid suggesting physical tension.

- **Russian** · охватывающая всю звуковысотную протяжённость двойной октавы
- **English** · spanning the full pitch range of the double octave
- **Evidence** · Vinet 50.14–51.3, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · V43–V45 and V50 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intentio, literal sense and increase [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:lat.ls.perseus-eng2-n24146/>
- **Confidence and review** · high · confirmed

**Divergence · duplicesque habens facultates**

Is duplex a twofold set of capacities or a doubling of their magnitude?

- **A, B, D** · The twofold compass contains two sets of capacities.
  - A · RU «обладает двоякими возможностями» / EN “having twofold powers”
  - B · RU «обладающая двоякими возможностями» / EN “has two kinds of capacity”
  - D · RU «Её возможности двояки» / EN “has a twofold capacity”
- **C** · Double capacities, leaving it possible to read a doubling of the capacity or of each capacity.
  - C · RU «имеющая двойные возможности» / EN “having double capacities”

**Verdict** · The explanatory eas … atque clause supplies two sets, the intervals already mentioned and the three larger ones. Twofold describes that division. This is ordinary duplex, so no ratio numeral should be supplied.

- **Russian** · обладающая двоякими возможностями
- **English** · with two sets of capacities
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 50.21–51.3 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. duplex I–I.B.1 [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=duplex> · about.md §4.3 [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · added

**Errors**

- **A · both · minor mistranslation** · “напряжение двойной октавы / tension of the double octave”. An abstract interval has a sounding compass here. Literal tension leaves the intended property obscure. **Fix** · Render the full pitch range of the double octave. *(confirmed)*
- **C · both · minor mistranslation** · “напряжение двойной октавы / tension of the double octave”. An abstract interval has a sounding compass here. Literal tension leaves the intended property obscure. **Fix** · Render the full pitch range of the double octave. *(confirmed)*
- **C · both · minor language** · “двойные возможности / double capacities”. This can suggest twice the capacity or a doubling of each capacity, rather than the two different sets listed at once in the Latin. **Fix** · двоякие возможности / two sets of capacities *(added)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · SYstematum autem hoc quidem Te- / trachordum est, quod omnes inter- / ualli Dià-tessáron continet facultates: / aliud verò Pentachordum, quod interual- / li Dià-pénte facultates complectitur: a- / liud Octachordum, quod totam interual- / li Dià-pasôn vim continet: aliud deinceps / Heckædecachordum, bis Dià-pasôn vni- / uersam comprehendens intensionem, du- / plicésque habens facultates: eas enim, / quas modò diximus, atque interuallorum / Dià-pason Dià-tessáron, et Dià-pason / Dià-pénte, et bis Dià-pason facultates in / se continet.
  - **Proposal** · …comprising the whole extent of the double octave… / …которая объемлет всё протяжение двойной октавы…
  - The alternative correctly states the extent contained within the system. Specifying pitch makes the alternative more informative.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet 50.14–51.3, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · V43–V45 and V50 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. intentio, literal sense and increase [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:lat.ls.perseus-eng2-n24146/>
- **C · doubt · open** (confirmed · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · SYstematum autem hoc quidem Te- / trachordum est, quod omnes inter- / ualli Dià-tessáron continet facultates: / aliud verò Pentachordum, quod interual- / li Dià-pénte facultates complectitur: a- / liud Octachordum, quod totam interual- / li Dià-pasôn vim continet: aliud deinceps / Heckædecachordum, bis Dià-pasôn vni- / uersam comprehendens intensionem, du- / plicésque habens facultates: eas enim, / quas modò diximus, atque interuallorum / Dià-pason Dià-tessáron, et Dià-pason / Dià-pénte, et bis Dià-pason facultates in / se continet.
  - **Proposal** · which contains all the properties of the interval of the fourth / который содержит все свойства интервала кварты
  - “Properties” is possible if it refers to functions available within the system, but facultas ordinarily connotes what the interval can admit or make possible, and the kind of properties remains unstated.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet 50.14–51.3, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. facultas, opening definitions [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:lat.ls.perseus-eng2-n17539/>
- **A · text issue · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · Dià-pason Dià-tessáron, et Dià-pason Dià-pénte (51.1–2), without cum and without the circumflex on pason
  - **Proposal** · Keep as printed.
  - Both compounds are printed without cum. Exactly the same intervals appear with cum in V35–V40, and the two expressions remain distinct items in this list.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 51, supplied scan [checked] · V35–V40 [checked]
- **B · text issue · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · Dià-pason Dià-tessáron, et Dià-pason / Dià-pénte
  - **Proposal** · Read both bare juxtapositions as compound interval names.
