**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Keep the distinction between facultas and vis and the contrast with Xylander’s account by string count.
- **B** · Note that Vinet here leaves cum out of both compound names though the surrounding list makes the compounds clear.

### V50 · Vinet 51.4–7

*Differunt autem Systemata ab intervallis quae in ipsis sunt, quod illa in materia consistunt: intervalla vero a materia abstracta consideramus.*

Systems subsist in matter; the intervals they contain are considered in abstraction from matter.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Keeps the distinction between material systems and the way intervals are considered.
- **English base** · B = D. “Subsist in matter” and “in abstraction” reproduce the scholastic distinction naturally.

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · Explain that strings are an example of the material instance and the interval is the pitch distance considered apart from them.

### V51 · Vinet 51.8–9

*Caput V. De Harmonia.*

Chapter V, On Harmony.

- **Russian base** · B = D. Both give the heading plainly.
- **English base** · B = D. Both give the heading plainly.

### V52 · Vinet 51.10–15

*Harmoniae porro proprium est Systemata illa componere aptareque ad aliquod genus modulationis, ut ad Diatonon, ad Chromaticon, et ad id quod ab ipsa harmonia cognominatum, Harmonicum dicimus.*

Harmony puts systems together and adjusts them to a genus of melody, namely the diatonic, chromatic or harmonic. The last is explicitly named after harmony itself.

- **Russian base** · D. Natural verbs and a clear account of the derivation.
- **English base** · D. Preserves the work of harmony and the explicit derivation of harmonic from harmony.

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Keep Vinet’s Greek forms Diatonon and Chromaticon and his later spellings, and explain the conventional harmonic for the genus ordinarily termed enharmonic.

### V53 · Vinet 51.15–20

*Si igitur tonis plurimum harmonia peragitur, Diatonon sic vocatum efficitur genus: si Hemitoniis, Chromaticum: si vero diesibus fere harmonia continetur, conficitur, quam Harmonicam appellant modulationem.*

Predominant tones yield the diatonic genus, predominant semitones the chromatic, and a preponderance of dieses the melody called harmonic.

- **Russian base** · D. Uses idiomatic Russian to state how each category arises.
- **English base** · D. Maintains the parallel sequence without implying that the steps in each genus are all of one size.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · open** (modified · for the editor)
  - **Printed** · nicum dicimus. Si igitur tonis plurimùm / harmonia peragitur, Diátonon sic voca- / tum efficitur genus: si Hemitoniis, Chro- / maticum: si verò diesibus ferè harmonia / continetur, conficitur, quam Harmonicam / appellant modulationem.
  - **Proposal** · (a) 'is for the most part made up of dieses'; (b) 'is held together almost wholly by dieses'.
  - Fere can mean almost or for the most part. The following omnino makes the almost/entire opposition grammatically possible, but 'almost wholly' implies a stronger numerical or spatial claim than the technical description supports. In V59 two of the three steps are dieses and cover one fifth of the stated compass. 'Chiefly' leaves the basis of predominance implicit.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 51, supplied scan [checked] · V54 and V59 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. fere I–II [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=fere>

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · The following three-interval divisions explain that two of the three steps are the named kind.

### V54 · Vinet 51.21–52.1

*Verumenimvero neque omnino tonis, quae ad Harmoniam pertinent, efferuntur, neque hemitoniis omnino, neque omnino diesibus: hoc enim fieri non potest:*

Harmonic material cannot be set out wholly in tones, wholly in semitones, or wholly in dieses.

- **Russian base** · B. Explicitly conveys the three parallel prohibitions.
- **English base** · B. Idiomatic and keeps the impossibility clause distinct.

### V55 · Vinet 52.1–6

*quinimo, quoniam ex tribus intervallis Dia-tessaron intervallum confectum est, duobusque tonis et hemitonio comprehensum, necesse est, postquam duo prima intervalla imposueris, reliquum esse Hemitonium:*

A fourth is made from three intervals and comprises two tones and a semitone. After placing the first two intervals, the semitone necessarily remains.

- **Russian base** · D. Makes the inference and the three constituents clear.
- **English base** · D. Uses the clearest construction while retaining the implicit assumption about the first two intervals.

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Contrast the omission of the small increment here with the account of the fourth at V39.

### V56 · Vinet 52.6–8

*atque hoc fuerit Dia-tessaron Diatonon cognominatum genus, ex Hemitonio et tonis constans duobus.*

The division just described is the diatonic genus of the fourth, consisting of one semitone and two tones.

- **Russian base** · D. Compact, idiomatic and faithful to the naming and contents.
- **English base** · A = C = D. Identical clear rendering of the naming and contents.

### V57 · Vinet 52.9–11

*Si vero duo sint hemitonia, reliquum fuerit unum ex confusione intervallum, Trihemitonium simplex:*

With two semitones set out, the remaining one-step interval arises by fusion and is a simple or incomposite trihemitone.

- **Russian base** · B. Makes it clear that the interval is traversed in one step.
- **English base** · B. Makes sense of the technical simplex while preserving the description of fusion.

**Divergence · Trihemitonium simplex**

Does simplex need an explicit technical rendering indicating an interval traversed as one step?

- **A, C, D** · Simple trihemitone, with technical sense explained in their notes.
  - A · RU «Если же полутонов два, остатком будет один интервал, [возникший] из слияния, — простой трёхполутон;» / EN “But if there are two semitones, the remainder will be one interval [arising] from blending, a simple trihemitone;”
  - C · RU «Если же будут два полутона, оставшимся будет один интервал, [образованный] слиянием, — простой трёхполутон;» / EN “But if there are two semitones, the remainder will be a single interval [formed] by fusion, the simple trihemitone;”
  - D · RU «Если же даны два полутона, то останется один интервал, возникший от слияния, — простой трёхполутон,» / EN “If, however, there are two semitones, what remains will be a single interval formed by blending, a simple trihemitone,”
- **B** · Incomposite trihemitone in the translation itself.
  - B · RU «Если же взять два полутона, то остаток составит один интервал, полученный слиянием, — несоставной трёхполутон,» / EN “If there are two semitones, the remainder will be a single interval formed by merging, an incomposite trihemitone,”

**Verdict** · B makes the specific sense evident through the contrast with V31–V32 and from unum here. A, C and D preserve Vinet’s lexical distinction from non-compositus when their notes supply the sense.

- **Russian** · несоставной трёхполутон
- **English** · an incomposite trihemitone
- **Evidence** · Vinet 52.9–11, reading and diplomatic lines [checked] · V31–V34 [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. confusio I [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=confusio> · about.md §4.2 [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · confirmed · for the editor

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Tie “fusion” to V32 and clarify that the trihemitone is one step even though its size is three semitones.

**Editor questions**

- If simplex is rendered “incomposite” here, explain that Vinet uses simplex here and non-compositus in V34.

### V58 · Vinet 52.11–14

*eritque id Dia-tessaron genus Chromaticum, ex Hemitoniis duobus et Trihemitonio temperatum, quod priore moestius luctuosiusque est.*

Two semitones and a trihemitone blended in due proportion form the chromatic genus of the fourth. This genus is sadder and more mournful than the preceding diatonic one.

- **Russian base** · D. The separate comparison sentence clearly refers to the genus.
- **English base** · D. Idiomatic account of compounding, with an explicit antecedent in the comparison.

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · Keep the common root of temperatum here and temperat in V60.
- **D** · Contrast Vinet’s account of the chromatic genus’s own character with Xylander’s description of the effects on the listener.

### V59 · Vinet 52.14–20

*Si vero per duas dieses modulatio peragatur, quod reliquum est intervallum, erit unum ditonium ex confusione, ac Dia-tessaron hoc genus Harmonicum nominatur, quod sit optimum, ab ipsa communi omnium harmonia cognominatum.*

If melodic movement uses two dieses, the remaining interval is one ditone formed by fusion. This genus of the fourth, regarded as the best, is named harmonic after harmony itself, described as common to all.

- **Russian base** · D. Good sequence and register, with the supplied referent visibly bracketed.
- **English base** · A. Keeps “common to all” open while linking the reason to the naming.

**Divergence · ab ipsa communi omnium harmonia**

All the genera, all things, or an unqualified all?

- **A** · Unqualified all, keeping the antecedent implicit.
  - A · RU «Если же мелодия ведётся через две диесы, то остающийся интервал будет одним дитоном, [возникшим] из слияния, и этот род кварты называется гармоническим: как наилучший, он назван по самой гармонии, общей для всех.» / EN “But if the melody is carried through two dieses, the interval that remains will be one ditone [arising] from blending, and this genus of the fourth is named harmonic: because it is the best, it has been named after harmony itself, which is common to all.”
- **B, D** · All the genera, with the addition bracketed.
  - B · RU «Если же мелодическое движение идёт по двум диесам, оставшийся интервал будет одним дитоном, образованным слиянием. Этот род кварты называется гармоническим. Он наилучший и потому получил своё имя от самой гармонии, общей для всех [родов].» / EN “If the melodic movement is carried out through two dieses, the remaining interval will be a single ditone formed by merging. This genus of the fourth is called harmonic. Because it is the best, it takes its name from harmony itself, which is common to all [the genera].”
  - D · RU «Если же мелодия движется по двум диесам, то оставшимся интервалом будет один дитон, возникший от слияния. Этот род кварты носит имя гармонического, потому что считается наилучшим. Имя он получил от самой гармонии, общей для всех [родов].» / EN “If the melody proceeds by two dieses, the remaining interval will be a single ditone formed by blending. This genus of the fourth is called harmonic on the ground that it is the best, taking its name from harmony itself, which all [the genera] share.”
- **C** · All things, taken as cosmic harmony.
  - C · RU «Если же мелодия проходит через две диесы, оставшийся интервал будет одним дитоном, [образованным] слиянием, и этот род кварты называется гармоническим как наилучший, получив прозвание от самой гармонии, общей для всего.» / EN “But if the melody proceeds through two dieses, the remaining interval will be a single ditone by fusion, and this genus of the fourth is called harmonic on the ground that it is the best, being named after the very harmony common to all things.”

**Verdict** · A retains the openness of omnium. BD draw on the immediate account of the genera at V52. C’s wider reading can recall the opening, but no substantive in this sentence forces it. The reason for the name is given as a ground through quod sit optimum.

- **Russian** · по самой гармонии, общей для всех
- **English** · after harmony itself, which is common to all
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 52, supplied scan [checked] · V05, V52 [checked] · Allen & Greenough §540, causal clauses [checked] <https://dcc.dickinson.edu/grammar/latin/causal-clauses>
- **Confidence and review** · medium · confirmed · for the editor

**Notes to keep**

- **C** · The note on the reason expressed in the subjunctive is useful if expressed as a possible distancing nuance.

**Editor questions**

- Decide how explicit to make omnium. The immediate antecedent is the genera, but the cosmic sense of harmony from V05 can be allowed to resonate.

### V60 · Vinet 52.21–22

*Eodem modo et caetera quoque systemata constituit harmonia, temperatque.*

Harmony constructs the remaining systems and proportions them in the same way.

- **Russian base** · D. Clear and consistent with the account of composition.
- **English base** · D. Duly proportions conveys the technical role of temperare without a later tuning implication.

### V61 · Vinet 52.23–27

*Ex proposito enim exemplo, quo ostensum in uno tetrachordo, quomodo intervalla intervalli Dia-tessaron constituantur, facile studiosus alia systemata quomodo componantur, assequi poterit.*

From the single tetrachord just used to illustrate how the component intervals of the fourth are formed, the diligent student can readily understand the construction of the other systems.

- **Russian base** · D. Clear handling of the constituent intervals and a natural sentence division.
- **English base** · B. “Constituent intervals of a fourth” communicates the relation without needless repetition.

### V62 · Vinet 53.1–4

*Genus autem Harmonicum est modulatu difficillimum, meditatione multa exercitationeque indigens: unde eius est usus rarior.*

The harmonic genus is the hardest to perform, needs much preparation and practice, and is therefore used less frequently.

- **Russian base** · C = D. Both express the difficulty and the distinct need for preparation and practice clearly.
- **English base** · C = D. Both translate the supine idiomatically and distinguish the two kinds of preparation.

**Notes to keep**

- **B** · Retain the brief explanation of the supine modulatu and the two terms for rehearsal and practice.

### V63 · Vinet 53.4–6

*Diatonum vero est simplum et generosum et magis naturale: quare id et Plato admittit.*

The diatonic genus is simple, noble and more natural, which is why Plato also admits it.

- **Russian base** · D. Marks the ellipsis of genus and uses idiomatic predicates.
- **English base** · B = D. Both preserve the comparative and Plato’s acceptance.

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · The Timaeus gives a mathematical parallel, and the named modes in the Republic address a different classification. Vinet cites no particular passage.
- **C** · Record simplus as distinct from the simplex of V57 and preserve the difference between Vinet’s Diatonum and Diatonon forms in the apparatus.

### V64 · Vinet 53.7–14

*Horum autem unumquodque rursus variatur, et in multas divisiones et differentias et genera modulationum distribuitur: et modo Lydius, modo Phrygius, modo Dorius appellatur modulus: a quorum quolibet Paeeon, Hymenaeus, Exodius, et quotquot sunt modulationum genera, existunt.*

Each genus is further varied and distributed among many divisions, differences and kinds of melody. The mode is called Lydian, Phrygian or Dorian. The paean, wedding song, departure song and all kinds of melody can arise from any of the modes.

- **Russian base** · D. Identifies the genera and mode, and makes the song examples intelligible without unexplained transliterations.
- **English base** · D. The mode and distinct song types are clear. “Song of departure” leaves the occasion open.

**Divergence · Hymenaeus, Exodius**

Do the generic labels enter as learned names or as intelligible descriptions of song types?

- **A, C** · Both names retained as learned forms.
  - A · RU «Каждый же из этих [родов], в свою очередь, видоизменяется и распределяется по многим делениям, различиям и родам мелодий; и лад называется то лидийским, то фригийским, то дорийским; а от каждого из них происходят пеан, гименей, эксодий и все, сколько их есть, роды мелодий.» / EN “Each of these, in turn, is varied and distributed into many divisions and differences and genera of melodies; and the mode is called now Lydian, now Phrygian, now Dorian; and from each of these arise the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius, and all the genera of melodies that there are.”
  - C · RU «Каждый же из них, в свою очередь, видоизменяется и распределяется на многие подразделения, различия и роды мелодий: лад называется то лидийским, то фригийским, то дорийским; и от любого из них происходят пеан, гименей, эксодий и все, сколько их ни есть, роды мелодий.» / EN “Each of these, in turn, is varied and distributed into many divisions and differences and genera of melody: the mode is called now Lydian, now Phrygian, now Dorian; and from any one of them arise the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius, and all the genera of melody there are.”
- **B** · Hymenaeus retained and Exodius translated as an exit or closing song.
  - B · RU «Каждый из них, в свою очередь, меняется и распадается на множество подразделений, разновидностей и родов мелодического движения. Лад называется то лидийским, то фригийским, то дорийским. Из любого из них возникают пеан, гименей, заключительная песнь и все прочие роды мелодического движения.» / EN “Each of these, in turn, varies and is distributed into many divisions, varieties, and genera of melodic movement. The mode is called now Lydian, now Phrygian, now Dorian. From any of these arise the paean, the hymenaeus, the exit song, and all the other genera of melodic movement.”
- **D** · Wedding song and song of departure, with “farewell song” in Russian.
  - D · RU «Каждый из этих [родов] опять-таки допускает изменения и распадается на множество подразделений, разновидностей и родов мелодии. Лад называют то лидийским, то фригийским, то дорийским. Из любого из них возникают пеан, свадебная песнь, прощальная песнь и все прочие роды мелодии, сколько их ни есть.» / EN “Each of these [genera] again admits of variation and is divided into many subdivisions, varieties and genera of melody. The mode is called now Lydian, now Phrygian, now Dorian. From any one of them arise the paean, the wedding song, the song of departure and all the genera of melody there are.”

**Verdict** · The wedding sense is secure. A generic departure sense best explains Exodius, and Pollux supplies a theatre parallel without restricting Vinet to a theatre or a funeral. D makes both informative and the Latin names can sit beside them in a note.

- **Russian** · свадебная песнь, песнь при выходе
- **English** · wedding song, song of departure
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 53.7–14 [checked] · Middle Liddell, s.v. ὑμέναιος, wedding song [checked] <https://atlas.perseus.tufts.edu/dictionaries/entry/urn:cite2:scaife-viewer:dictionary-entries.atlas_v1:middle-liddell.perseus-eng2-n33476/> · LSJ and Pape, s.v. ἐξόδιος, exit and finale, with Pape citing Pollux IV.108 [checked] <https://cld.bbaw.de/lemma/grc/%E1%BC%90%CE%BE%CF%8C%CE%B4%CE%B9%CE%BF%CF%82> · about.md §4.1 [checked]
- **Confidence and review** · high · modified

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · unsound** (modified)
  - **Printed** · Horum autem vnumquodque rursus / variatur, et in multas diuisiones et diffe- / rentias et genera modulationum distribui / tur: et modò Lydius, modò Phrygius, mo- / dò Dorius appellatur modulus: à quorum / quolibet Pæéon, Hymenæus, Exodius, et / quotquot sunt modulationum genera, exi / stunt. Non enim sola interuallorum mu-
  - **Proposal** · (a) 'from each of these [modes]'; (b) 'from each of these [genera]'.
  - The inflection of quorum and quolibet allows a masculine or neuter antecedent. The nearest governing idea is modulus with the three alternatives just enumerated, and the concrete mode names are the natural source in the narrative sequence. The remoter three genera are grammatically possible, but not the best context.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 53.7–14 [checked] · Allen & Greenough §§303, 305 and 306b [checked] <https://dcc.dickinson.edu/grammar/latin/relative-pronouns> · Lewis & Short, s.v. modulus I.4 [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=modulus>
- **B · doubt · sound** (modified)
  - **Printed** · Horum autem vnumquodque rursus / variatur, et in multas diuisiones et diffe- / rentias et genera modulationum distribui / tur: et modò Lydius, modò Phrygius, mo- / dò Dorius appellatur modulus: à quorum / quolibet Pæéon, Hymenæus, Exodius, et / quotquot sunt modulationum genera, exi / stunt. Non enim sola interuallorum mu-
  - **Proposal** · departure song / песнь при выходе
  - Departure and exit both retain the unspecific scope of the Latin term. The dramatic parallel supports the act of going out, while a broader “closing song” has less lexical precision.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon (1591), supplied scan 53.7–14 [checked] · LSJ and Pape, s.v. ἐξόδιος, exit and finale, with Pape citing Pollux IV.108 [checked] <https://cld.bbaw.de/lemma/grc/%E1%BC%90%CE%BE%CF%8C%CE%B4%CE%B9%CE%BF%CF%82>
- **D · doubt · unsound** (modified)
  - **Printed** · Horum autem vnumquodque rursus / variatur, et in multas diuisiones et diffe- / rentias et genera modulationum distribui / tur: et modò Lydius, modò Phrygius, mo- / dò Dorius appellatur modulus: à quorum / quolibet Pæéon, Hymenæus, Exodius, et / quotquot sunt modulationum genera, exi / stunt. Non enim sola interuallorum mu-
  - **Proposal** · funeral song / погребальная песнь
  - The Latin word points to departure with no expressed funeral setting. Greek usage permits a dramatic finale as one occasion. The translator’s Holy Saturday reference is not verified, and the possibility of that usage would still not narrow this passage to a funeral.

  - **Proposal** · Keep the reading Paeeon.

- **B** · Use the Pliny parallel to explain modulus and Pollux for the theatrical example of a song sung on exit.

### V65 · Vinet 53.14–20

*Non enim sola intervallorum mutatione, neque sola eorum differentia: sed etiam lentioribus, et concitatis transitionibus, pausis quoque et fluxibus, atque variis tactibus pulsibusque, pro vario manus temperamento, infinitae et diversae modulationes conficiuntur.*

There are limitless varieties of melody. Their means include changes and differences in intervals, slower and rapid transitions, pauses and flow, and varied touches and strokes according to the player’s control of the hand.

- **Russian base** · D. Distinguishes rests from continuous movement and communicates the contribution of the hand.
- **English base** · B. Makes the lists readable, with idiomatic “flowing movements” and “touches and strokes”.

**Divergence · pro vario manus temperamento**

The measure of the hand, or the player’s varying control of it?

- **A, B, D** · The hand’s mode of operation or varying control, with a more literal Russian version in A.
  - A · RU «Ибо не одной только переменой интервалов и не одним только их различием, но также более медленными и стремительными переходами, равно как паузами и течениями, и разнообразными касаниями и ударами, сообразно различной мере [движения] руки, создаются бесчисленные и разнообразные мелодии.» / EN “For it is not by change of the intervals alone, nor by their difference alone, but also by slower and by rapid transitions, by pauses too and flowings, and by various touches and strokes, according to the varying control of the hand, that infinite and diverse melodies are produced.”
  - B · RU «Бесчисленные и разнообразные мелодические движения получаются не только от перемены интервалов или от различий между ними, но также от более медленных и стремительных переходов, пауз и плавных движений, разных касаний и ударов, в зависимости от того, как действует рука.» / EN “Countless and varied forms of melodic movement are produced by changes and differences of interval, and also by slower and more rapid transitions, pauses and flowing movements, and various touches and strokes, depending on how the hand is controlled.”
  - D · RU «Не только смена интервалов и различия между ними порождают бесконечное множество самых разных мелодий. К этому ведут и переходы, то более медленные, то стремительные, и паузы, и непрерывное движение, и разнообразные прикосновения и удары, смотря по тому, как ими управляет рука.» / EN “For melodies of infinite number and variety are produced by changes of interval and by differences between intervals, but also by slower and more animated transitions, by pauses and passages of flowing movement, and by varied touches and strokes according to the varying control of the hand.”
- **C** · The varied measure of the hand, with movements supplied in Russian.
  - C · RU «Ведь не одним только изменением интервалов и не одним только их различием, но и более медленными и стремительными переходами, а также паузами и течениями, и различными касаниями и ударами, сообразно различной мере [движений] руки, создаются бесчисленные и разнообразные мелодии.» / EN “For not by the change of intervals alone, nor by their difference alone, but also by slower and quicker transitions, by pauses too and flowings, and by various touches and strokes, according to the varied measure of the hand, are infinite and diverse melodies produced.”

**Verdict** · The nearby touches and strokes indicate the player’s varying control of the hand. C preserves a lexical value of temperamentum, though an unexplained measure of the hand is liable to be read as a size or unit.

- **Russian** · в зависимости от того, как исполнитель управляет движениями руки
- **English** · according to the varying control of the hand
- **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 53, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. temperamentum [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=temperamentum>
- **Confidence and review** · high · confirmed

**Errors**

- **C · en · minor language** · “according to the varied measure of the hand”. Measure of the hand is unclear as a description of the execution of the touches and strokes. **Fix** · according to the varying control of the hand *(confirmed)*
- **D · en · minor language** · “produced by changes of interval and by differences between intervals, but also by”. But also is unpaired with a preceding limiting expression. The two sole clauses should anchor its force. **Fix** · produced not only by changes and differences of interval but also by *(confirmed)*
- **B · en · minor omission** · “are produced by changes and differences of interval, and also by”. The two occurrences of sola and their reversal by sed etiam distinguish two insufficient explanations from supplementary factors. A flat list of causes preserves the factors but loses that explicit structure. **Fix** · are produced not only by changes and differences of interval, but also by *(added)*

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A · doubt · unsound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · stunt. Non enim sola interuallorum mu- / tatione, neque sola eorum differentia: sed / etiam lentioribus, et concitatis transitio- / nibus, pausis quoqne et fluxibus, atque va- / riis tactibus pulsibúsque, pro vario manus / temperamento, infinitæ et diuersæ mo- / dulationes conficiuntur.
  - **Proposal** · …according to the varied tuning by the hand… / …сообразно различной настройке, [производимой] рукой…
  - The immediate setting is the hand’s touches and strokes during performance. No words identify a tuning operation or address string pitches individually.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 53, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. temperamentum [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=temperamentum>
- **C · doubt · sound** (confirmed)
  - **Printed** · stunt. Non enim sola interuallorum mu- / tatione, neque sola eorum differentia: sed / etiam lentioribus, et concitatis transitio- / nibus, pausis quoqne et fluxibus, atque va- / riis tactibus pulsibúsque, pro vario manus / temperamento, infinitæ et diuersæ mo- / dulationes conficiuntur.
  - **Proposal** · according to the varied management of the hand / смотря по различному владению рукой
  - The contextual alternative expresses the hand’s role in regulating touches and strokes, which the bare “measure of the hand” leaves opaque.
  - **Evidence** · Vinet, Tournon 1591, p. 53, supplied scan [checked] · Lewis & Short, s.v. temperamentum [checked] <https://alatius.com/ls/index.php?l=temperamentum>

**Notes to keep**

- **D** · Keep the hand’s execution as the context of touches and strokes without identifying an instrument the Latin leaves unstated.
- **B** · Compare Vinet’s slower transitions with Xylander’s oblique transitions.

### V66 · Vinet 53.21

*Finis Musicae.*

End of the section on music.

- **Russian base** · A = C = D. Preserves the concise closing formula and marks the section name as a title.
- **English base** · B. The bracketed clarification makes the section’s status clear.

| C | составные / несоставными | composite / incomposite |

**Recommended** · составной / несоставной интервал / composite / incomposite interval. Retain the switch from compositae at X26 to incomposita in X27.

about.md §§4.1–4.2 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.4, I.7–8, I.16, I.20–23, ed. Friedlein [checked] <https://la.wikisource.org/wiki/De_musica_(Boethius,_ed._Friedlein)/Liber_primus> · Latin at X26, X27, X29, X42, X43, X49 [checked]

### simplex / simplicia / non-composita · vinet

V31, V32, V34, V57

| Translator | Russian | English |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | простой | simple |

| D | несоставные | incomposite |

**Recommended** · простой / несоставной / simple / incomposite. Use simple for simplex where the wording matters and explain its technical equivalence to an interval sounded as one step. V34 explicitly negates non-composita.

about.md §§4.1–4.2 [checked] · Boethius, De institutione musica I.4, I.7–8, I.16, I.20–23, ed. Friedlein [checked] <https://la.wikisource.org/wiki/De_musica_(Boethius,_ed._Friedlein)/Liber_primus> · Latin at V31, V32, V34, V57 [checked]

| Translator | Russian | English |

**Recommended** · величина полости / размеры трубок / size of the cavity / dimensions of the pipes. Xylander expressly specifies a cavity. Vinet names the pipe. Both allow the physical dimension to remain open in the running text.

### Term inconsistencies

- **B · numeri sonorum and related expressions** · X37 uses «количества звуков или струн», whereas X38 specifies «[порядковый номер]» and «номера предшествующих звуков». The connected proof compares position numbers. X39’s «число звуков» also allows the misleading reading of a count.
- **B · proportionalitas** · X04 gives «пропорциональность» and “proportionality”, while X32 gives «взаимных пропорциях» and “their mutual proportions”. Keep this term distinct from the nearby ratio vocabulary and from proportio.
- **D · proportionalitas** · X04 gives «пропорциональность» and “proportionality”, while X32 gives «во взаимных пропорциях» and “in their mutual proportions”. A phrase for corresponding proportions can render the plural while retaining the distinction from proportio.
- **B · genus, ordinary classification** · English X56 has “any other genera of melody” and X57 “kinds of tune” in the same discussion of types of song. A consistent word for ordinary kinds would distinguish both passages from the technical three genera.
- **D · genus, ordinary classification** · X56 has «роды мелодий» and “genera of melodies”, while X57 has «виды напевов» and “kinds of tune”. In Russian the latter also loses the distinction from species, rendered «видов» earlier at X56.
- **B · vis** · V32 has “the force of many intervals” and V49 “the entire capacity of the octave”. The latter merges vis with facultas, also rendered “capacity”, precisely where the two words alternate.

## Overall assessment

### A

- **Strengths** · Preserves marked source wording and often distinguishes nearby Latin terms. The notes on the inclusive ratios in V22 and the divergences between the additive counts and exact ratios in V39 are useful. A keeps V59’s common to all open and draws attention to the distinct forms of the compound ratio at X36 and V40.
- **Weaknesses** · Close English calques can change a technical assertion, most clearly in saying that eight and thirty-two cannot be squared at V27. At X07 a note turns the gifts of music into a threefold classification. A’s doubt at V17 incorrectly makes mese the only single-word name in the list. Tension also becomes opaque where intensio denotes pitch or the compass of an interval, especially at V45 and V49.

### B

- **Strengths** · Usually gives readable technical prose and consistently exposes the arithmetic behind the ratio brackets. The rendering of duplo acutius at V22 makes clear that the size of an upward step is doubled. B handles the pitch compass at V49 and the semitone-versus-single-step distinction at V57 well.
- **Weaknesses** · Explanatory recasting sometimes changes the structure of the example. X15 suggests one physical instrument contains another, the English at X28 loses part of the pairing of names, and the Russian at X37 uses the number of sounds or strings as a count where the argument needs ordinal positions. The English at V49 gives vis the same equivalent as facultates, obscuring Vinet’s change of word.

### C

- **Strengths** · Tracks lexical differences between the two Latin versions and gives especially detailed accounts of the printed diagrams. The explicit fraction at V42 and the Paeeon note at V64 are particularly useful. C regularly preserves awkward features of the reading text and supplies interpretive alternatives separately.
- **Weaknesses** · Some literal or learned terms make distinctions less accessible in either language, as with melos at V32 and the measure of the hand at V65. English commensurability at V03–V05 is narrower than the context requires. The claim about the unique status of mese at V17 is false. The running text can also commit to an interpretation the syntax leaves open, as with cosmic harmony at V59.

### D

- **Strengths** · Often gives the strongest reading base in both languages. D is particularly clear on pitch and accord in V11 and V15, the contrast between the ratios of pitches and positions at V44, and the difference between intervals and material systems. D’s V27 note correctly distinguishes division in two from an unrestricted partition into equal squares.
- **Weaknesses** · Idiomatic supplementation sometimes makes a choice that requires brackets or narrows the Latin, as with the instrument at V18 and the explicit causal connection in Russian at V39. The V42 note gives an inferred count among the printed numerals. In the performance vocabulary, X57’s Russian makes fluxus specifically uninterrupted, and V65’s English loses the limiting expression that supports but also.

### Summary

D and B most often provide the clearest running prose. A and C offer especially useful notes on verbal distinctions, the arithmetic and the printing. The best base varies by segment and by language. In the opening, preserve Xylander’s commensuratio and proportionalitas beside Vinet’s symmetria and proportio. In the definitions, let the reader see the difference between Xylander’s participation in harmony and Vinet’s capacity to accord. Keep the expressly different note names and explain the shared eighth position behind the fifteen- and sixteen-string counts. Distinguish the additive pitches marked in Vinet’s diagram from exact equal divisions of a ratio. Keep the anomalous remainder in the fourth and explain how it relates to the later tetrachord division. The editor should review the proposed readings at X23–X24, the timing argument at X32 and V37, the status of quod at V18 and the scope of omnium at V59. A note should identify the ratios and the actual visible marks in the diagrams and distinguish any supplied identifications. Claims about sources that the reviewers could not open remain unverified.

### Contamination

none
